\displaystyle x_{e_{1}-e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{e_{2}+e_{3}}(-t)

\displaystyle x_{-e_{1}+e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{-e_{2}-e_{3}}(-t)

\displaystyle x_{e_{2}-e_{3}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{e_{2}-e_{3}}(t)

\displaystyle x_{-e_{2}+e_{3}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{-e_{2}+e_{3}}(t)

\displaystyle x_{e_{2}-e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{e_{2}-e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle x_{-e_{2}+e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{-e_{2}+e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle x_{e_{3}-e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{e_{3}-e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle x_{-e_{3}+e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{-e_{3}+e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle x_{e_{1}+e_{2}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{-e_{1}+e_{2}}(t)

\displaystyle x_{-e_{1}-e_{2}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{e_{1}-e_{2}}(t)

\displaystyle x_{e_{1}+e_{3}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{-e_{1}+e_{3}}(t)

\displaystyle x_{-e_{1}-e_{3}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{e_{1}-e_{3}}(t)

\displaystyle x_{e_{1}+e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{-e_{1}+e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle x_{-e_{1}-e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{e_{1}-e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle x_{e_{2}+e_{3}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{-e_{1}-e_{4}}(-t)

\displaystyle x_{-e_{2}-e_{3}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{e_{1}+e_{4}}(-t)

\displaystyle x_{e_{2}+e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{-e_{1}-e_{3}}(t)

\displaystyle x_{-e_{2}-e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{e_{1}+e_{3}}(t)

\displaystyle x_{e_{3}+e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{-e_{1}-e_{2}}(-t)

\displaystyle x_{-e_{3}-e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto x_{e_{1}+e_{2}}(-t)

Proof.

We demonstrate one of the calculations for
x_{\alpha}(t)
with
\alpha\notin\Delta
. Throughout this calculation we use the commutator relations computed in
[
Rue20
, Appendix 2]
. We can express
x_{e_{1}-e_{2}}(t)
in terms of commutators as

x_{e_{1}-e_{2}}(t)=\big((x_{e_{1}+e_{3}}(t),x_{-e_{3}+e_{4}}(-1)),x_{-e_{2}-e_{4}}(1)\big).

This therefore gets mapped to the element

\displaystyle\big((x_{\theta^{+}(e_{1}+e_{3})}(t),x_{\theta^{+}(-e_{3}+e_{4})}(-1)),x_{\theta^{+}(-e_{2}-e_{4})}(1)\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big((x_{-e_{1}+e_{3}}(t),x_{-e_{3}+e_{4}}(-1)),x_{e_{1}+e_{3}}(1)\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(x_{-e_{1}+e_{4}}(t),x_{e_{1}+e_{3}}(1))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x_{e_{3}+e_{4}}(-t)

as noted above. The other calculations proceed similarly.
∎

Let
\theta
be the reflection of the graph which exchanges
e_{1}-e_{3}
and
-e_{1}-e_{3}
. Since this stabilizes the intermediate lattice associated to
\mathbf{O}_{8}^{+}
, we also have a well defined automorphism
\theta\colon\mathbf{O}_{8}^{+}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{O}_{8}^{+}
. As shown in the following lemma, this recovers the familiar fact that

\mathbf{Aut}(\mathbf{O}_{8}^{+})=\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{2}\cong\mathbf{PGO}_{8}.

Lemma 2.2
.

Let
\varphi\in\mathbf{O}_{8}
be the automorphism of
(\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{V}),q_{8})
which sends
v_{1}\mapsto-v_{1}^{*}
and
v_{1}^{*}\mapsto-v_{1}
while fixing all other basis elements. Note
\varphi\notin\mathbf{O}_{8}^{+}
.

(i)

The automorphism
\theta\colon\mathbf{Spin}_{8}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
is the restriction of
\mathbf{C}(\varphi)
.

(ii)

The automorphism
\theta\colon\mathbf{O}_{8}^{+}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{O}_{8}^{+}
is the restriction of the inner automorphism
\Inn(\varphi)
of
\mathbf{O}_{8}
.

(iii)

We have that
\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\in\mathbf{PGO}_{8}
, but not in
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
, and
\theta\colon\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
is the restriction of the inner automorphism
\Inn(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi))
of
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}
.

Proof.

(i)
: We can compute the following.

\mathbf{C}(\varphi)(X_{e_{1}-e_{3}}(t))=\mathbf{C}(\varphi)(1+tv_{1}v_{3}^{*})=1-tv_{1}^{*}v_{3}^{*}=1+tv_{3}^{*}v_{1}^{*}=X_{-e_{1}-e_{3}}(t).

Because
\varphi
is order
2
, so is
\mathbf{C}(\varphi)
, and so we also have
\mathbf{C}(\varphi)(X_{-e_{1}-e_{3}}(t))=X_{e_{1}-e_{3}}(t)
. Further, since
X_{e_{2}+e_{4}}(t)
and
X_{e_{3}-e_{4}}(t)
do not involve
v_{1}
or
v_{1}^{*}
, they are fixed by
\mathbf{C}(\varphi)
. Therefore,
\mathbf{C}(\varphi)
agrees with
\theta
on
X_{\alpha}(t)
for
\alpha\in\Delta
and therefore it agrees with
\theta
on all of
\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
as claimed.

(ii)
: Using our identification
\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{V}))\cong\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})
, the automorphism
\Inn(\varphi)
acts on the basis elements of
\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})
by

\displaystyle E_{1j}

\displaystyle\mapsto-E_{\bar{1}j}

\displaystyle E_{\bar{1}j}

\displaystyle\mapsto-E_{1j}

\displaystyle E_{j1}

\displaystyle\mapsto-E_{j\bar{1}}

\displaystyle E_{j\bar{1}}

\displaystyle\mapsto-E_{j1}

\displaystyle E_{11}

\displaystyle\mapsto E_{\bar{1}\bar{1}}

\displaystyle E_{\bar{1}\bar{1}}

\displaystyle\mapsto E_{11}

\displaystyle E_{1\bar{1}}

\displaystyle\mapsto E_{\bar{1}1}

\displaystyle E_{\bar{1}1}

\displaystyle\mapsto E_{1\bar{1}}

while fixing all other basis elements. This has the effect of mapping
x_{e_{1}-e_{3}}(t)\mapsto x_{-e_{1}-e_{3}}(t)
and
x_{-e_{1}-e_{3}}(t)\mapsto x_{e_{1}-e_{3}}(t)
while fixing
x_{-e_{2}+e_{3}}(t)
and
x_{e_{2}+e_{4}}(t)
. As above, since it agrees with
\theta
on these Chevalley generators of
\mathbf{O}_{8}^{+}
, we conclude that
\Inn(\varphi)
restricted to
\mathbf{O}_{8}^{+}
is
\theta
.

(iii)
: The claim about
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
follows because
x^{\prime}_{\alpha}(t)=\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(x_{\alpha}(t))
, and so in
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}
we will have

\displaystyle\Inn(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi))(x^{\prime}_{\alpha}(t))

\displaystyle=\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\circ x^{\prime}_{\alpha}(t)\circ\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)^{-1}=\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi\circ x_{\alpha}(t)\circ\varphi^{-1})

\displaystyle=\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\theta(x_{\alpha}(t)))=\theta(x^{\prime}_{\alpha}(t)),

which finishes the proof.
∎

2.B.
Clifford Triality

Let
\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{O}^{4}
and consider the regular hyperbolic quadratic form
(\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{V}),q_{8})
as in
Example

1.1
. Notationally, let
(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}),\sigma_{8},f_{8})\cong(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{V})),\sigma_{q_{8}},f_{q_{8}})
be the adjoint quadratic triple with the isomorphism coming from our standard choice of ordered basis for
\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{V})
. We have the isomorphism

\Phi_{0}\colon(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell_{0}(\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{V}),q_{8}),\underline{\sigma}_{0},\underline{f})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\wedge_{0}\mathcal{V}),\sigma_{\wedge 0},f_{\wedge 0})\times(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\wedge_{1}\mathcal{V}),\sigma_{\wedge 1},f_{\wedge 1})

of
Example

1.1
, where
(\sigma_{\wedge i},f_{\wedge i})
are the adjoint quadratic pairs of the quadratic form
q_{\wedge}
restricted to each component. We choose the following ordered bases of
\wedge_{0}\mathcal{V}
and
\wedge_{1}\mathcal{V}
.

(13)

\displaystyle\{1,v_{\{1,2\}},v_{\{1,3\}},v_{\{1,4\}},-v_{\{2,3\}},v_{\{2,4\}},-v_{\{3,4\}},v_{[4]}\}

\displaystyle\text{ of }\wedge_{0}\mathcal{V},\text{ and}

\displaystyle\{-v_{\{2,3,4\}},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4},-v_{\{1,2,3\}},v_{\{1,2,4\}},-v_{\{1,3,4\}},-v_{1}\}

\displaystyle\text{ of }\wedge_{1}\mathcal{V}.

These choices provide isomorphisms of quadratic modules
(\wedge_{i}\mathcal{V},q_{\wedge i})\cong(\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{V}),q_{8})
. In turn, we have isomorphisms
(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\wedge_{i}\mathcal{V}),\sigma_{\wedge i},f_{\wedge i})\cong(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}),\sigma_{8},f_{8})
. Therefore, by applying these isomorphisms and our standard isomorphism
(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}),\sigma_{8},f_{8})\cong(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{V})),\sigma_{q_{8}},f_{q_{8}})
, the isomorphism
\Phi_{0}
becomes an isomorphism

(14)

\Psi\colon(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}),\sigma_{8},f_{8}),\underline{\sigma_{8}},\underline{f_{8}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}),\sigma_{8},f_{8})\times(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}),\sigma_{8},f_{8}).

We now analyze the homomorphism

\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{PGO}_{8}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell}\mathbf{Aut}(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}),\sigma_{8},f_{8}),\underline{\sigma_{8}},\underline{f_{8}})

where
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell
is the map of
Equation

7
. By definition of
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
, the image of this homomorphism will consist of automorphisms which fix the center of the Clifford algebra. In our case, the isomorphism
\Psi
makes it clear that this center is
\mathcal{O}\times\mathcal{O}
. Any automorphism which fixes the center then also stabilizes each factor of the decomposition
(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}),\sigma_{8},f_{8})\times(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}),\sigma_{8},f_{8})
. Therefore, we get a homomorphism

\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\to\mathbf{PGO}_{8}\times\mathbf{PGO}_{8}.

We describe this homomorphism explicitly using Chevalley generators. Recall that by
Equation

6
, the image under
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell
of any
\varphi\in\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
is the inner automorphism of the Clifford algebra given by any element
X\in\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
lying above
\varphi
. Therefore, we compute the images of the Chevalley generators of
\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
from
Section

1.C.ii
under the homomorphism
\Psi
.

Lemma 2.3
.

The images of the Chevalley generators of
\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
under the homomorphism
\Psi
of
Equation

14
are as follows.

\displaystyle X_{e_{1}-e_{2}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{e_{3}+e_{4}}(-t),x_{-e_{1}-e_{2}}(t))

\displaystyle X_{-e_{1}+e_{2}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{-e_{3}-e_{4}}(-t),x_{e_{1}+e_{2}}(t))

\displaystyle X_{e_{1}-e_{3}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{e_{2}+e_{4}}(t),x_{-e_{1}-e_{3}}(t))

\displaystyle X_{-e_{1}+e_{3}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{-e_{2}-e_{4}}(t),x_{e_{1}+e_{3}}(t))

\displaystyle X_{e_{1}-e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{e_{2}+e_{3}}(-t),x_{-e_{1}-e_{4}}(t))

\displaystyle X_{-e_{1}+e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{-e_{2}-e_{3}}(-t),x_{e_{1}+e_{4}}(t))

\displaystyle X_{e_{2}-e_{3}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{e_{2}-e_{3}}(t),x_{e_{2}-e_{3}}(t))

\displaystyle X_{-e_{2}+e_{3}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{-e_{2}+e_{3}}(t),x_{-e_{2}+e_{3}}(t))

\displaystyle X_{e_{2}-e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{e_{2}-e_{4}}(t),x_{e_{2}-e_{4}}(t))

\displaystyle X_{-e_{2}+e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{-e_{2}+e_{4}}(t),x_{-e_{2}+e_{4}}(t))

\displaystyle X_{e_{3}-e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{e_{3}-e_{4}}(t),x_{e_{3}-e_{4}}(t))

\displaystyle X_{-e_{3}+e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{-e_{3}+e_{4}}(t),x_{-e_{3}+e_{4}}(t))

\displaystyle X_{e_{1}+e_{2}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{-e_{1}+e_{2}}(t),x_{-e_{3}-e_{4}}(-t))

\displaystyle X_{-e_{1}-e_{2}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{e_{1}-e_{2}}(t),x_{e_{3}+e_{4}}(-t))

\displaystyle X_{e_{1}+e_{3}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{-e_{1}+e_{3}}(t),x_{-e_{2}-e_{4}}(t))

\displaystyle X_{-e_{1}-e_{3}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{e_{1}-e_{3}}(t),x_{e_{2}+e_{4}}(t))

\displaystyle X_{e_{1}+e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{-e_{1}+e_{4}}(t),x_{-e_{2}-e_{3}}(-t))

\displaystyle X_{-e_{1}-e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{e_{1}-e_{4}}(t),x_{e_{2}+e_{3}}(-t))

\displaystyle X_{e_{2}+e_{3}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{-e_{1}-e_{4}}(-t),x_{e_{1}-e_{4}}(-t))

\displaystyle X_{-e_{2}-e_{3}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{e_{1}+e_{4}}(-t),x_{-e_{1}+e_{4}}(-t))

\displaystyle X_{e_{2}+e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{-e_{1}-e_{3}}(t),x_{e_{1}-e_{3}}(t))

\displaystyle X_{-e_{2}-e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{e_{1}+e_{3}}(t),x_{-e_{1}+e_{3}}(t))

\displaystyle X_{e_{3}+e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{-e_{1}-e_{2}}(-t),x_{e_{1}-e_{2}}(-t))

\displaystyle X_{-e_{3}-e_{4}}(t)

\displaystyle\mapsto(x_{e_{1}+e_{2}}(-t),x_{-e_{1}+e_{2}}(-t))

Proof.

We demonstrate the calculation of one image and the other calculations proceed similarly. Consider
X_{e_{1}-e_{2}}(t)=1+tv_{1}v_{2}^{*}\in\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
. The element
v_{1}v_{2}^{*}\in\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell_{0}(\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{V}),q_{8})
maps via
\Phi
to
\ell_{v_{1}}\circ d_{v_{2}^{*}}\in\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\wedge\mathcal{V})
. We calculate how this endomorphism behaves on the chosen bases of
Equation

13
. It sends

\displaystyle-v_{\{2,3\}}

\displaystyle\mapsto-v_{\{1,3\}}

\displaystyle-v_{\{2,3,4\}}

\displaystyle\mapsto-v_{\{1,3,4\}}

\displaystyle v_{\{2,4\}}

\displaystyle\mapsto v_{\{1,4\}}

\displaystyle v_{2}

\displaystyle\mapsto v_{1}

and maps all other bases elements to zero. Using the isomorphisms
\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\wedge_{i}\mathcal{V})\cong\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})
coming from the ordered bases, this endomorphism becomes the matrices

(-E_{3,\bar{4}}+E_{4,\bar{3}},E_{\bar{2},1}-E_{\bar{1},2}).

Therefore,

X_{e_{1}-e_{2}}(t)\mapsto(I+(-t)(E_{3,\bar{4}}-E_{4,\bar{3}}),I+t(E_{\bar{2},1}-E_{\bar{1},2}))=(x_{e_{3}+e_{4}}(-t),x_{-e_{1}-e_{2}}(t))

as claimed above.
∎

Corollary 2.4
.

The homomorphism

\mathbf{PGO}_{8}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell}\mathbf{Aut}(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}),\sigma_{8},f_{8}),\underline{\sigma_{8}},\underline{f_{8}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Aut}\big((\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}),q_{8},f_{8})\times(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}),q_{8},f_{8})\big)

restricts to the homomorphism

\displaystyle\Psi^{+}\colon\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}

\displaystyle\to\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\times\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}

\displaystyle\varphi

\displaystyle\mapsto(\theta^{+}(\varphi),\theta^{-}(\varphi)).

Proof.

Since all of the images described in
Lemma

2.3
lie in
\mathbf{O}_{8}^{+}\times\mathbf{O}_{8}^{+}
, all of
\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
must also map into this group. Therefore, when we consider the inner automorphisms by these elements we see that the map between adjoint groups will behave as, for example,

x^{\prime}_{e_{1}-e_{2}}(t)\mapsto(x^{\prime}_{e_{3}+e_{4}}(-t),x^{\prime}_{-e_{1}-e_{2}}(t))

and likewise on other Chevalley generators according to
Lemma

2.3
. Therefore it is clear we get a map
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\to\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\times\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
which by comparison of the images of the Chevalley generators with
Lemma

2.1
, we see is
\varphi\mapsto(\theta^{+}(\varphi),\theta^{-}(\varphi))
as claimed.
∎

Remark 2.5
.

The reader will have noticed that our choice of ordered basis for
\wedge_{0}\mathcal{V}
is a natural one with respect to which
q_{\wedge 0}
is obviously hyperbolic. With this natural choice of basis, the automorphism
\theta^{+}
appears in the first factor while performing computations, and so this natural choice of basis for
\wedge_{0}\mathcal{V}
justifies the less natural choice of simple system for
D_{4}
. The simple system we use is equivalent (after the relabelling
(0,1,2,3)\to(1,3,2,4)
) to the simple system obtained in
[
EK13
, Fig. 6.1]
in the context of triality computations for Lie algebras of type
D_{4}
.

If one uses the natural ordered basis

\{v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4},-v_{\{1,2,3\}},v_{\{1,2,4\}},-v_{\{1,3,4\}},v_{\{2,3,4\}}\}

of
\wedge_{1}\mathcal{P}
, the automorphism
\theta^{-}
does not appear in the second factor. Instead,
\theta\circ\theta^{-}
appears. This justifies why we use a less natural basis of
\wedge_{1}\mathcal{V}
.

Corollary 2.6
.

Consider the element
\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\in\mathbf{PGO}_{8}
of
Lemma

2.2
. Using our standard identification
\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{V}))\cong\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})
, we view this as an automorphism of
\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})
. Then, the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}),\sigma_{8},f_{8})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})\times\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}),\sigma_{8},f_{8})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})\times\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sw}\circ(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\times\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi}

where
\mathrm{sw}\colon\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})\times\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})\to\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})\times\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})
is the switch isomorphism,
\mathrm{sw}(b,c)=(c,b)
.

Proof.

Since the elements
X_{\alpha}(1)-1
, where
X_{\alpha}(1)
is a Chevalley generator of
\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
, generate
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}),\sigma_{8},f_{8})
as an algebra, it is sufficient to argue that

\Psi\big(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi))(X_{\alpha}(1))\big)=(\mathrm{sw}\circ(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\times\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi))\circ\Psi)(X_{\alpha}(1))

for all
\alpha\in D_{4}
. However,
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi))=\mathbf{C}(\varphi)
which by
Lemma

2.2
(i)
acts on elements of
\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
by
\theta\colon\mathbf{Spin}_{8}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
. One can then inspect the table of
Lemma

2.3
to see that

\Psi(\theta(X_{\alpha}(1)))=(\mathrm{sw}\circ(\theta\times\theta)\circ\Psi)(X_{\alpha}(1))

holds for all
\alpha\in D_{4}
, which is equivalent to the desired formula above. This completes the proof.
∎

2.C.
Triality with Octonions

Here we compare our preceding development of triality to the usual method using octonions. First, we will give a brief outline of the development over a scheme in terms of a general symmetric composition algebra analogous to what is done in
[
KMRT
, §35]
over fields of characteristic not
2
,
[
DQ21
, §4]
over a field of characteristic
2
, or
[
AG19
, §3.2]
over any ring. The first two sources also describe triality for similitudes, however in this section we will only describe triality for adjoint groups. Then, by applying this machinery to the split para-Cayley algebra and carefully choosing a basis, we show that we obtain the same order
3
outer automorphisms of
\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{8}
as above.

2.C.i.
Symmetric Composition Algebras

To begin, we follow the definitions given in
[
KMRT
, §34]
, adapting them over schemes. Let
(\mathcal{S},n)
be a finite locally free non-associative
\mathcal{O}
–algebra with a regular multiplicative quadratic form
n\colon\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{O}
. I.e.,
n
is regular and denoting the multiplication in
\mathcal{S}
by
x\star y
, we have
n(x\star y)=n(x)n(y)
. The quadratic form
n
has an associated polar bilinear form
b_{n}(x,y)=n(x+y)-n(x)-n(y)
and we call
(\mathcal{S},n)
a
symmetric composition algebra
if

b_{n}(x\star y,z)=b_{n}(x,y\star z)

for all appropriate sections
x,y,z\in\mathcal{S}
. This is equivalent to
(\mathcal{S},n)
satisfying the relations

x\star(y\star x)=n(x)y=(x\star y)\star x

for all appropriate
x,y\in\mathcal{S}
.

Now, assume that
(\mathcal{S},n)
is a symmetric composition algebra of constant rank
8
. Since symmetric composition algebras come with a quadratic form, we may discuss their Clifford algebra,
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{S},n)
, and their even Clifford algebra,
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell_{0}(\mathcal{S},n)\cong\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S}),\sigma_{n},f_{n})
, where
(\sigma_{n},f_{n})
is the quadratic pair adjoint to
n
. There is an isomorphism

\displaystyle\alpha\colon\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{S},n)

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S}\oplus\mathcal{S})

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\mapsto\begin{bmatrix}0&\ell_{x}\\
r_{x}&0\end{bmatrix}

defined on the image of
\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{S},n)
and extended algebraically. Here,
\ell_{x}(y)=x\star y
and
r_{x}(y)=y\star x
are the left and right multiplication by
x
operators respectively. This isomorphism restricts to the even Clifford algebra and the restriction respects canonical involutions, see
[
AG19
, 3.10]
for the result over rings, giving an isomorphism

\alpha_{0}\colon(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell_{0}(\mathcal{S},n),\underline{\sigma_{n}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S}),\sigma_{n})\times(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S}),\sigma_{n}).

The following lemma is
[
DQ21
, 4.5]
over a field of characteristic
2
. We adapt their proof.

Lemma 2.7
.

The isomorphism
\alpha_{0}
respects the canonical semi-trace on
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell_{0}(\mathcal{S},n)
. That is, we have an isomorphism of algebras with semi-trace

\alpha_{0}\colon(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell_{0}(\mathcal{S},n),\underline{\sigma_{n}},\underline{f_{n}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S}),\sigma_{n},f_{n})\times(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S}),\sigma_{n},f_{n})

Proof.

It is sufficient to show that this hold locally over a cover. Therefore, by considering a fine enough cover such that everything splits, we may assume that
\mathcal{S}
is free,
n
is hyperbolic, and that
\underline{f_{n}}=\Trd_{\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell_{0}(\mathcal{S},n)}(c(a)\underline{\hskip 8.61108pt})
for an element
a\in\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S})
with
\Trd_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S})}(a)=1
. Let
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{1}^{*}\}
be a hyperbolic basis of
\mathcal{S}
with respect to
n
. Under the isomorphism
\varphi_{n}\colon\mathcal{S}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{S}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S})
of
Equation

1
,
\varphi_{n}(v_{1}^{*}\otimes v_{1})
sends
v_{1}\mapsto v_{1}
and all other basis elements to zero, and therefore has trace
1
. We use the element
c(\varphi_{n}(v_{1}^{*}\otimes v_{1}))\in\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S}),\sigma_{n},f_{n})
to define
\underline{f_{n}}
. Viewing it as an element in
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell_{0}(\mathcal{S},n)
, it appears simply as
v_{1}^{*}v_{1}
.

The semi-trace
\underline{f_{n}}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}
is the map
\Trd_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S})\times\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S})}(\alpha_{0}(v_{1}^{*}v_{1})\underline{\hskip 8.61108pt})
. We compute that

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}(v_{1}^{*}v_{1})

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&\ell_{v_{1}^{*}}\\
r_{v_{1}^{*}}&0\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}0&\ell_{v_{1}}\\
r_{v_{1}}&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\ell_{v_{1}^{*}}r_{v_{1}}&0\\
0&r_{v_{1}^{*}}\ell_{v_{1}}\end{bmatrix}.

Hence, we only need to check that
\Trd_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S})}(\ell_{v_{1}^{*}}r_{v_{1}}\underline{\hskip 8.61108pt})
and
\Trd_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S})}(r_{v_{1}^{*}}\ell_{v_{1}}\underline{\hskip 8.61108pt})
agree with
f_{n}
. Since the image of any semi-trace is determined on symmetrized elements, it is sufficient to consider symmetric elements of the form
\varphi_{n}(v_{i}\otimes v_{i})
and
\varphi_{n}(v_{i}^{*}\otimes v_{i}^{*})
. We demonstrate these computations in the first case. Let
v
be any basis vector.

\displaystyle\Trd_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S})}(\ell_{v_{1}^{*}}r_{v_{1}}\varphi_{n}(v\otimes v))

\displaystyle=\Trd_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S})}(\varphi_{n}(v\otimes v)\ell_{v_{1}^{*}}r_{v_{1}})

\displaystyle=\Trd_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S})}(b_{n}(v,\underline{\hskip 8.61108pt})v\circ\ell_{v_{1}^{*}}\circ r_{v_{1}})

\displaystyle=\Trd_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S})}(b_{n}(v,v_{1}^{*}\star(\underline{\hskip 8.61108pt}\star r_{v_{1}}))v)

\displaystyle=b_{n}(v,v_{1}^{*}\star(v\star v_{1}))

\displaystyle=b_{n}(v\star v_{1}^{*},v\star v_{1})

\displaystyle=n(v\star v_{1}^{*}+v\star v_{1})-n(v\star v_{1}^{*})-n(v\star v_{1})

\displaystyle=n(v)\big(n(v_{1}^{*}+v_{1})-n(v_{1}^{*})-n(v_{1})\big)

\displaystyle=n(v)\cdot 1

\displaystyle=n(v)

where we use the multiplicativity of
n
. Likewise, in the second case

\displaystyle\Trd_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S})}(r_{v_{1}^{*}}\ell_{v_{1}}\varphi_{n}(v\otimes v))

\displaystyle=\Trd_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S})}(b_{n}(v,\underline{\hskip 8.61108pt})v\circ r_{v_{1}^{*}}\circ\ell_{v_{1}})

\displaystyle=\Trd_{\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.20552pt\textit{n\hskip-0.60275ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S})}(b_{n}(v,(v_{1}\star\underline{\hskip 8.61108pt})\star v_{1}^{*})v)

\displaystyle=b_{n}(v,(v_{1}\star v)\star v_{1}^{*})

\displaystyle=b_{n}((v_{1}\star v)\star v_{1}^{*},v)

\displaystyle=b_{n}(v_{1}\star v,v_{1}^{*}\star v)

\displaystyle=b_{n}(v_{1},v_{1}^{*})n(v)

\displaystyle=n(v).

Since these agree with the defining property of
f_{n}
, we conclude that
\alpha_{0}
respects the semi-traces as claimed.
∎

Triality then arises from the isomorphism
\alpha_{0}
as follows. For an automorphism
\varphi\in\mathbf{PGO}^{+}(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S}),\sigma_{n},f_{n})
we obtain an automorphism
\alpha_{0}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\varphi)\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}
of
\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S})\times\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S})
which fixes the center and hence stabilizes the factors. Therefore, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell_{0}(\mathcal{S},n),\underline{\sigma_{n}},\underline{f_{n}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S}),\sigma_{n},f_{n})\times(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S}),\sigma_{n},f_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell_{0}(\mathcal{S},n),\underline{\sigma_{n}},\underline{f_{n}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S}),\sigma_{n},f_{n})\times(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S}),\sigma_{n},f_{n})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell(\varphi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{2}\times\varphi_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{0}}

where
\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}\in\mathbf{PGO}^{+}(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S}),\sigma_{n},f_{n})
. Defining
\theta_{\mathrm{oct}}^{+}(\varphi)=\varphi_{2}
and
\theta_{\mathrm{oct}}^{-}(\varphi)=\varphi_{3}
produces order three outer automorphisms of
\mathbf{PGO}^{+}(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{S}),\sigma_{n},f_{n})
with
(\theta_{\mathrm{oct}}^{+})^{2}=\theta_{\mathrm{oct}}^{-}
. In the following section we will consider Chevalley generators to identify these automorphisms and align them with the automorphisms
\theta^{+}
and
\theta^{-}
defined in
Section

2.A
which arise in
Corollary

2.4
.

2.C.ii.
The Split para-Cayley Algebra

The split Cayley algebra, i.e., the split octonions, can be produced from the matrix algebra
\Mat_{2}(\mathcal{O})
via the Cayley-Dickson construction. In particular, the split Cayley algebra is
\mathcal{C}_{s}=\mathrm{CD}(\Mat_{2}(\mathcal{O}),-1)
. The construction goes as follow, from
[
KMRT
, §33.C]
.

The matrix algebra
\Mat_{2}(\mathcal{O})
, being a quaternion algebra, has its canonical symplectic involution

\psi\left(\begin{bmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{bmatrix}\right)=\begin{bmatrix}d&-b\\
-c&a\end{bmatrix}.

This involution satisfies the identities

a+\psi(a)=\Trd_{\Mat_{2}(\mathcal{O})}(a)\cdot I\text{, and }a\cdot\psi(a)=\det(a)\cdot I

for all
a\in\Mat_{2}(\mathcal{O})
. This gives
\Mat_{2}(\mathcal{O})
the structure of a Hurwitz algebra. Then, introducing a symbol
\nu
,

\mathcal{C}_{s}=\mathrm{CD}(\Mat_{2}(\mathcal{O}),-1)=\Mat_{2}(\mathcal{O})\oplus\nu\Mat_{2}(\mathcal{O})

with component wise addition and with multiplication given by

(a+\nu b)(a^{\prime}+\nu b^{\prime})=aa^{\prime}-b^{\prime}\psi(b)+\nu\big(\psi(a)b^{\prime}+a^{\prime}b\big).

This comes with an involution
\overline{a+\nu b}=\psi(a)-\nu b
and a hyperbolic quadratic form
n(a+\nu b)=\det(a)+\det(b)
. These satisfy
x\cdot\overline{x}=n(x)
for
x\in\mathcal{C}_{s}
. The split Cayley algebra has what is called a
good basis
in
[
EK13
]
, for example comprised of the elements

\displaystyle e_{1}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle u_{1}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
0&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle u_{2}

\displaystyle=\nu\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
0&-1\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle u_{3}

\displaystyle=\nu\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
1&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle e_{2}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle v_{1}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
-1&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle v_{2}

\displaystyle=\nu\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle v_{3}

\displaystyle=\nu\begin{bmatrix}0&-1\\
0&0\end{bmatrix}.

The multiplication table for
\mathcal{C}_{s}
with respect to this basis is given in
[
EK13
, Figure 4.1]
. The involution takes the form
\overline{e_{1}}=e_{2}
,
\overline{u_{i}}=-u_{i}
, and
\overline{v_{i}}=-v_{i}
. Defining the para-multiplication by

x\star y=\overline{x}\cdot\overline{y}

for
x,y\in\mathcal{C}_{s}
makes
(\mathcal{C}_{s},\star,n)
a symmetric composition algebra of rank
8
, called the
split para-Cayley algebra
. By the previous section, we then have an isomorphism

\alpha_{0}\colon(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell_{0}(\mathcal{C}_{s},n),\underline{\sigma_{n}},\underline{f_{n}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{C}_{s}),\sigma_{n},f_{n})\times(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{C}_{s}),\sigma_{n},f_{n}).

In order to align the triality which arises from
\alpha_{0}
with the preceding results of this paper, we identify
\mathbf{PGO}^{+}(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{C}_{s}),\sigma_{n},f_{n})
with
\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{8}
by fixing the ordered hyperbolic basis

\{e_{1},-v_{2},-v_{1},-v_{3},-u_{3},-u_{1},-u_{2},e_{2}\}

of
\mathcal{C}_{s}
. This also allows us to identify
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell_{0}(\mathcal{C}_{s},n)
to
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell_{0}(\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{V}),q_{8})
of
Example

1.1
and therefore speak of the Chevalley generators of
\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
inside
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell_{0}(\mathcal{C}_{s},n)
or of Chevalley generators of
\mathbf{O}^{+}_{8}
inside
\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{C}_{s})
. Now, we may trace the images of these Chevalley generators through the isomorphism
\alpha_{0}
in order to identify the resulting automorphism
\theta_{\mathrm{oct}}^{+}
.

Lemma 2.8
.

The images of the Chevalley generators of
\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
in
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell_{0}(\mathcal{C}_{s},n)
under the isomorphism
\alpha_{0}
agree with the images stated in
Lemma

2.3
.

Proof.

This result is purely computational. We demonstrate one such computation for the first generator, the others proceed analogously.

The
\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
generator
X_{e_{1}-e_{2}}(t)=1+tv_{1}v_{2}^{*}\in\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell_{0}(\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{V}),q_{8})
takes the form

1+te_{1}(-u_{2})=1-te_{1}u_{2}

in
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell_{0}(\mathcal{C}_{s},n)
and we have that

\alpha_{0}(e_{1}u_{2})=\begin{bmatrix}\ell_{e_{1}}\circ r_{u_{2}}&0\\
0&r_{e_{1}}\circ\ell_{u_{2}}\end{bmatrix}.

We then compute, using the para-multiplication on
\mathcal{C}_{s}
, that the linear map
\ell_{e_{1}}\circ r_{u_{2}}
sends

-u_{3}\mapsto-v_{1}\text{ and }-u_{1}\mapsto v_{3}

and all other basis vectors to zero. Using the ordered basis, this is the matrix
E_{3,\overline{4}}-E_{4,\overline{3}}\in\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{C}_{s})
. Similarly, the linear map
r_{e_{1}}\circ\ell_{u_{2}}
sends

e_{1}\mapsto u_{2}\text{ and }-v_{2}\mapsto e_{2}

and all other basis vectors to zero. This corresponds to the matrix
-E_{\overline{2},1}+E_{\overline{1},2}
.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}(X_{e_{1}-e_{2}}(t))

\displaystyle=(I-t(E_{3,\overline{4}}-E_{4,\overline{3}}),I-t(-E_{\overline{2},1}+E_{\overline{1},2}))

\displaystyle=(I+(-t)(E_{3,\overline{4}}-E_{4,\overline{3}}),I+t(E_{\overline{2},1}-E_{\overline{1},2}))

\displaystyle=(x_{e_{3}+e_{4}}(-t),x_{-e_{1}-e_{2}}(t))

as claimed in
Lemma

2.3
.
∎

Corollary 2.9
.

Identifying
\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{8}\cong\mathbf{PGO}^{+}(\mathcal{E}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{n\hskip-0.86108ptd}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{C}_{s}),\sigma_{n},f_{n})
as above, we have that
\theta_{\mathrm{oct}}^{+}=\theta^{+}
and therefore also
\theta_{\mathrm{oct}}^{-}=\theta^{-}
.

Proof.

Since the computations in
Lemma

2.8
agree with those in
Lemma

2.3
, the group homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{8}

\displaystyle\to\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{8}\times\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{8}

\displaystyle\varphi

\displaystyle\mapsto(\theta_{\mathrm{oct}}^{+}(\varphi),\theta_{\mathrm{oct}}^{-}(\varphi))

arising from
\alpha_{0}
will agree with the map
\Psi^{+}
of
Corollary

2.4
. Hence
\theta_{\mathrm{oct}}^{+}=\theta^{+}
as claimed.
∎

3.
Trialitarian Triples

Following
[
KMRT
, 42.A]
, we define a groupoid of trialitarian triples which is equivalent to the category of
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
–torsors. Our definition of trialitarian triple, while given over schemes, is essentially identical to the definition given in
[
BT23
, §4]
where the authors use
[
DQ21
]
in order to define trialitarian triples over fields of any characteristic. We assemble these groupoids of trialitarian triples into a stack equivalent to the stack of
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
–torsors. Throughout this section, we abbreviate a quadratic triple using the notation
(\mathcal{A},\sigma_{\mathcal{A}},f_{\mathcal{A}})=\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}
.

3.A.
The Groupoid and the Gerbe

Definition 3.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
be the category whose

(i)

objects are quadruples
(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})
where the first three terms are each quadratic triples of degree
8
over
S
and the last term is an isomorphism

\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}\colon(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{\mathcal{A}}},\underline{f_{\mathcal{A}}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}

of
\mathcal{O}
–algebras with semi-trace, and whose

(ii)

morphisms are triples

(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})\colon(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})\to(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}})

where
\phi_{1}\colon\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}
,
\phi_{2}\colon\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}
, and
\phi_{3}\colon\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}
are isomorphisms of quadratic triples such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{\mathcal{A}}},\underline{f_{\mathcal{A}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}},\underline{f_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell(\phi_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{2}\times\phi_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}}

commutes.

If
(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})\in\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
, then clearly
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}})
is a Cartesian product of two Azumaya algebras and therefore the involution
\sigma_{\mathcal{A}}
of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}
is of trivial discriminant. Let
\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}=(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}),\sigma_{8},f_{8})
be the adjoint quadratic triple of the regular quadratic form
(\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{V}),q_{8})
of
Example

1.1
. The quadruple

(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}},\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}},\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}},\Psi),

where
\Psi
is the isomorphism of
Equation

14
, is an object of
\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
. We call it the
split object
and denote it by
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3}
.

We may similarly define a groupoid
\mathfrak{Trip}(T)
for any
T\in\Sch_{S}
, and these groupoids can be assembled into the fibers of a stack.

Definition 3.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{Trip}
be the following category.

(i)

The objects are pairs
(T,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))
consisting of
T\in\Sch_{S}
and
(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})\in\mathfrak{Trip}(T)
.

(ii)

The morphisms are pairs

(g,\phi)\colon(T^{\prime},(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}))\to(T,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))

where
g\colon T^{\prime}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}T
is an isomorphism of
S
–schemes and

\phi\colon(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}})\to(g^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}),g^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}),g^{*}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}),g^{*}(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))

is an isomorphism in
\mathfrak{Trip}(T^{\prime})
. Here we use the identification

(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(g^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}})),\underline{g^{*}(\sigma_{\mathcal{A}})},\underline{g^{*}(f_{\mathcal{A}})})=g^{*}(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{\mathcal{A}}},\underline{f_{\mathcal{A}}})

to consider
g^{*}(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})
as a map with the appropriate domain. Composition is given by
(g_{1},\phi_{1})\circ(g_{2},\phi_{2})=(g_{1}\circ g_{2},g_{2}^{*}(\phi_{1})\circ\phi_{2})
.

(iii)

We equip
\mathfrak{Trip}
with a structure functor
p\colon\mathfrak{Trip}\to\Sch_{S}
sending

(T,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))\mapsto T\text{ and }(g,\phi)\mapsto g

on objects and morphisms respectively.

Given a morphism
g\colon T^{\prime}\to T
of
S
–schemes and
(T,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))\in\mathfrak{Trip}
, the morphism

(g,\Id)\colon(T^{\prime},(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}|_{T^{\prime}}))\to(T,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))

is cartesian and makes the object
(T^{\prime},(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}|_{T^{\prime}}))
a pullback of
(T,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))
. We will use the notation
(T,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))|_{T^{\prime}}
to refer to this specific pullback. Therefore, the structure functor
p\colon\mathfrak{Trip}\to\Sch_{S}
makes
\mathfrak{Trip}
a fibered category whose fiber over
T
is the groupoid
\mathfrak{Trip}(T)
.

Lemma 3.3
.

Consider the fibered category
p\colon\mathfrak{Trip}\to\Sch_{S}
of
Definition

3.2
.

(i)

p\colon\mathfrak{Trip}\to\Sch_{S}
is a gerbe.

(ii)

The automorphism sheaf of the split object
(S,\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3})\in\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
is

\mathbf{Aut}(S,\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3})\cong\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}.

Proof.

(i)
: First, we must argue that
p\colon\mathfrak{Trip}\to\Sch_{S}
is a stack. To see that the
\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}
functors are sheaves, consider two objects in the same fiber,

(T,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})),(T,(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}))\in\mathfrak{Trip}(T)

for some
T\in\Sch_{S}
. The functor
\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}})\colon\Sch_{T}\to\Sets
of internal isomorphisms between
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}
and
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}
is a sheaf. Likewise
\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}})
and
\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}})
are sheaves. Consider the two morphisms

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}})\times\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}})\times\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}})

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}))

\displaystyle\rho_{1}\colon(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{1})

\displaystyle\rho_{2}\colon(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})

\displaystyle\mapsto\alpha_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}^{-1}\circ(\phi_{2}\times\phi_{3})\circ\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}.

The functor
\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}((T,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})),(T,(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}})))
is the equalizer of the two morphisms
\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{2}
between sheaves, and is therefore a sheaf itself as desired.

To see that
\mathfrak{Trip}
allows gluing of objects, let
T\in\Sch_{S}
and let
\{T_{i}\to T\}_{i\in I}
be a cover. Consider objects
(T_{i},(\mathcal{A}_{i\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{i\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{i\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}))\in\mathfrak{Trip}
with isomorphisms

\rho_{ij}\colon(T_{ij},(\mathcal{A}_{i\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{ij}},\mathcal{B}_{i\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{ij}},\mathcal{C}_{i\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{ij}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}|_{T_{ij}}))\overset{\sim}{\to}(T_{ij},(\mathcal{A}_{j\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{ij}},\mathcal{B}_{j\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{ij}},\mathcal{C}_{j\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{ij}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}_{j}}|_{T_{ij}}))

satisfying
\rho_{jk}\circ\rho_{ij}=\rho_{ik}
over
T_{ijk}
. First consider the quadratic triples
\mathcal{A}_{i\mathrm{qt}}
. The isomorphisms
\rho_{ij}
contain isomorphisms
(\phi_{1})_{ij}\colon\mathcal{A}_{i\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{ij}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{A}_{j\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{ij}}
satisfying
(\phi_{1})_{jk}\circ(\phi_{1})_{ij}=(\phi_{1})_{ik}
over
T_{ijk}
. Therefore, since quadratic triples allow gluing, there exists a quadratic triple
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}
over
T
and isomorphisms
\gamma_{\mathcal{A},i}\colon\mathcal{A}_{i\mathrm{qt}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}}
such that
(\gamma_{\mathcal{A},j})|_{T_{ij}}^{-1}\circ\gamma_{\mathcal{A},i}|_{T_{ij}}=(\phi_{1})_{ij}
. Similarly, there will be quadratic triples
\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}
over
T
with isomorphisms
\gamma_{\mathcal{B},i}\colon\mathcal{B}_{i\mathrm{qt}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}}
and
\gamma_{\mathcal{C},i}\colon\mathcal{C}_{i\mathrm{qt}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}}
. The isomorphisms
\alpha_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}
are also compatible on overlaps via the isomorphisms
\rho_{ij}
by assumption, and therefore they glue into an isomorphism
\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}\colon(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{\mathcal{A}}},\underline{f_{\mathcal{A}}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}
which makes
(T,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))
an object in
\mathfrak{Trip}(T)
. Locally there will be isomorphisms

\gamma_{i}=(\Id,(\gamma_{\mathcal{A},i},\gamma_{\mathcal{B},i},\gamma_{\mathcal{C},i}))\colon(T_{i},(\mathcal{A}_{i\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{i\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{i\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}))\overset{\sim}{\to}(T_{i},(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}|_{T_{i}}))

which by construction satisfy the relation
(\gamma_{j})|_{T_{ij}}^{-1}\circ\gamma_{i}|_{T_{ij}}=\rho_{ij}
. Hence, we see that
(T,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))
is a glued object as required and so
\mathfrak{Trip}
is a stack.

Towards showing that
\mathfrak{Trip}
is a gerbe, since
\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
is non-empty it is clear that all fibers are non-empty and so every object trivially has a cover over which the fibers are non-empty. It is also clear from construction that
\mathfrak{Trip}
is fibered in groupoids. Hence, we must only show that all objects in a fiber are twisted forms of one another. We show that for any
T\in\Sch_{S}
, every object
(T,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))\in\mathfrak{Trip}(T)
is a twisted form of the split object
(T,(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T},\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T},\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T},\Psi|_{T}))=(T,\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3}|_{T})\in\mathfrak{Trip}(T)
. Since quadratic triples are locally split, there exists a cover
\{T_{i}\to T\}_{i\in I}
such that for each
i\in I
there is an isomorphism
\phi_{1,i}\colon\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}}
. We consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{\mathcal{A}}},\underline{f_{\mathcal{A}}})|_{T_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}}\times\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{8}},\underline{f_{8}})|_{T_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}}\times\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell(\phi_{1,i})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\mathcal{A}|_{T_{i}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi|_{T_{i}}}

There are two cases. If
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{1,i})(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}|_{T_{i}}^{-1}(1,0))=\Psi|_{T_{i}}^{-1}(1,0)
and likewise for
(0,1)
, then defining
\phi_{2,i}\colon\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}}\to\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}}
by

(\phi_{2,i}(b),0)=(\Psi\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{1,i})\circ\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}^{-1})(b,0)

and similarly for
\phi_{3,i}\colon\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}}\to\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}}
will define an isomorphism

(\Id,(\phi_{1,i},\phi_{2,i},\phi_{3,i}))\colon(T_{i},(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}|_{T_{i}}))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(T_{i},\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3}|_{T_{i}})

in
\mathfrak{Trip}(T_{i})
as desired. However, in the second case we will have

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{1,i})(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}|_{T_{i}}^{-1}(1,0))

\displaystyle=\Psi|_{T_{i}}^{-1}(0,1)\text{, and}

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{1,i})(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}|_{T_{i}}^{-1}(0,1))

\displaystyle=\Psi|_{T_{i}}^{-1}(1,0).

Here, we may use the improper isometry
\varphi\in\mathbf{O}_{8}
with
\varphi\notin\mathbf{O}_{8}^{+}
of
Lemma

2.2
and instead consider the isomorphism
\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)|_{T_{i}}\circ\phi_{1,i}\colon\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}}\to\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}}
. Since
\varphi
is improper, the isomorphism
\mathbf{C}(\varphi)=\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi))
will swap the factors in the Clifford algebra and therefore return us to the first case. We then proceed as above to finish the proof and conclude that
\mathfrak{Trip}
is a gerbe.

(ii)
: For
T\in\Sch_{S}
, a section of
\mathbf{Aut}(S,\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3})(T)
is an automorphism of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3}|_{T}
in
\mathfrak{Trip}(T)
. Let
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})\in\Aut_{\mathfrak{Trip}(T)}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3}|_{T})
. Since the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{8}},\underline{f_{8}})|_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T}\times\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{8}},\underline{f_{8}})|_{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T}\times\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi|_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell(\phi_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{2}\times\phi_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi|_{T}}

commutes, we know from
Corollary

2.4
that we must have
\phi_{2}=\theta^{+}(\phi_{1})
and
\phi_{3}=\theta^{-}(\phi_{1})
. Therefore, the isomorphism is completely determined by the choice of
\phi_{1}\in\mathbf{PGO}_{8}(T)
, which must be an isomorphism such that
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{1})
preserves the order of the factors in the Clifford algebra. This is the definition of
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
and therefore gives a clear isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathbf{Aut}(S,\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3})(T)

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}(T)

\displaystyle(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})

\displaystyle\mapsto\phi_{1}.

These isomorphisms clearly assemble into an isomorphism of sheaves

\mathbf{Aut}(S,\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}

as desired.
∎

Lemma

3.3
means that
\mathfrak{Trip}
is equivalent to the gerbe of
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
–torsors. In particular, for every object
(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})\in\mathfrak{Trip}
, the sheaf of isomorphisms

\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3},(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))

is a
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
–torsor and this provides the equivalence of stacks. Looking globally, this means we have an equivalence of categories between the category of
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
–torsors over
S
and
\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
. In terms of cohomology, we view
H^{1}(S,\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+})
as the set of isomorphism classes in
\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
.

3.B.
Endofunctors of
\mathfrak{Trip}(S)

We now define an action of the abstract group
\mathbb{S}_{3}
as endofunctors on the groupoid
\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
. Let
(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})\in\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
. Notice that the isormorphism
\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}
is uniquely determined by the
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
–torsor
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3},(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))
. Indeed, let
\{T_{i}\to S\}_{i\in I}
be a cover over which there are sections
(\phi_{i1},\phi_{i2},\phi_{i3})\in\mathcal{P}(T_{i})
. Commutativity of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{8}},\underline{f_{8}})|_{T_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}|_{T_{i}})_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}|_{T_{i}})_{\mathrm{qt}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{\mathcal{A}}},\underline{f_{\mathcal{A}}})|_{T_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}}\times\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi|_{T_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell(\phi_{i1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{i2}\times\phi_{i3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}|_{T_{i}}}

means that

(15)

\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}|_{T_{i}}=(\phi_{i2}\times\phi_{i3})\circ\Psi|_{T_{i}}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{i1})^{-1}.

Also, this formula does not depend on the choice of isomorphism
(\phi_{i1},\phi_{i2},\phi_{i3})
. We now consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{8}},\underline{f_{8}})|_{T_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}|_{T_{i}})_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}|_{T_{i}})_{\mathrm{qt}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{\mathcal{B}}},\underline{f_{\mathcal{B}}})|_{T_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}}\times\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi|_{T_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell(\phi_{i2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{i3}\times\phi_{i1}}

and define
\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}|_{T_{i}}
to match the dashed map, in particular

(16)

\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}|_{T_{i}}=(\phi_{i3}\times\phi_{i1})\circ\Psi|_{T_{i}}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{i2})^{-1}.

In the following lemma, we argue that these local definitions do not depend on the choice of isomorphism in
\mathcal{P}(T_{i})
and that they glue into a well-defined isomorphism
\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}\colon(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{\mathcal{B}}},\underline{f_{\mathcal{B}}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}
.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})
be an object in
\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
and set

\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3},(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})).

(i)

For all
T\in\Sch_{S}
such that there are isomorphisms
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3}),(\phi_{1}^{\prime},\phi_{2}^{\prime},\phi_{3}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{P}(T)
, we have
\phi_{i}^{-1}\circ\phi_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}(T)
for each
i=1,2,3
and furthermore

\displaystyle\phi_{2}^{-1}\circ\phi_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\theta^{+}(\phi_{1}^{-1}\circ\phi_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\phi_{3}^{-1}\circ\phi_{3}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\theta^{-}(\phi_{1}^{-1}\circ\phi_{1}^{\prime}).

(ii)

For all
T\in\Sch_{S}
as in
(i)
, the maps

\displaystyle(\phi_{3}\times\phi_{1})\circ\Psi|_{T}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{2})^{-1},\text{ and}

\displaystyle(\phi^{\prime}_{3}\times\phi^{\prime}_{1})\circ\Psi|_{T}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi^{\prime}_{2})^{-1}

are equal.

(iii)

There exists a unique isomorphism
\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}\colon(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{\mathcal{B}}},\underline{f_{\mathcal{B}}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}
such that for all
T\in\Sch_{S}
as in
(i)
, we have

\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}|_{T}=(\phi_{3}\times\phi_{1})\circ\Psi|_{T}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{2})^{-1}.

Proof.

(i)
: Using
Equation

15
with each isomorphism, and then taking inverses on the first equation, we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{1})\circ\Psi|_{T}^{-1}\circ(\phi_{2}^{-1}\times\phi_{3}^{-1}),\text{ and}

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle=(\phi^{\prime}_{2}\times\phi^{\prime}_{3})\circ\Psi|_{T}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi^{\prime}_{1})^{-1}.

This of course implies that

\Id_{\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}})}=\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{1})\circ\Psi|_{T}^{-1}\circ(\phi_{2}^{-1}\times\phi_{3}^{-1})\circ(\phi^{\prime}_{2}\times\phi^{\prime}_{3})\circ\Psi|_{T}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi^{\prime}_{1})^{-1}

which rearranges to

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{1}^{-1})\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle=\Psi|_{T}^{-1}\circ(\phi_{2}^{-1}\times\phi_{3}^{-1})\circ(\phi^{\prime}_{2}\times\phi^{\prime}_{3})\circ\Psi|_{T}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{1}^{-1}\circ\phi^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle=\Psi|_{T}^{-1}\circ((\phi_{2}^{-1}\circ\phi_{2}^{\prime})\times(\phi_{3}^{-1}\circ\phi_{3}^{\prime}))\circ\Psi|_{T}.

This shows that
\phi_{1}^{-1}\circ\phi_{1}^{\prime}
preserves the center of the Clifford algebra and therefore is an element of
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
. The final two claimed formulas then follow from
Corollary

2.4
and this also implies that
\phi_{i}^{-1}\circ\phi_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
for
i=2,3
.

(ii)
: Using the formulas from
(i)
, we know that
\theta^{+}(\phi_{2}^{-1}\circ\phi_{2}^{\prime})=\phi_{3}^{-1}\circ\phi_{3}^{\prime}
and
\theta^{-}(\phi_{2}^{-1}\circ\phi_{2}^{\prime})=\phi_{1}^{-1}\circ\phi_{1}^{\prime}
. Therefore by
Corollary

2.4
,

\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{2}^{-1}\circ\phi_{2}^{\prime})=\Psi|_{T}^{-1}\circ((\phi_{3}^{-1}\circ\phi_{3}^{\prime})\times(\phi_{1}^{-1}\circ\phi_{1}^{\prime}))\circ\Psi|_{T}.

We rearrange this in the reverse manner as above to obtain that

\Id_{\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}})}=\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{2})\circ\Psi|_{T}^{-1}\circ(\phi_{3}^{-1}\times\phi_{1}^{-1})\circ(\phi^{\prime}_{3}\times\phi^{\prime}_{1})\circ\Psi|_{T}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi^{\prime}_{2})^{-1}

meaning that
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{2})\circ\Psi|_{T}^{-1}\circ(\phi_{3}^{-1}\times\phi_{1}^{-1})
and
(\phi^{\prime}_{3}\times\phi^{\prime}_{1})\circ\Psi|_{T}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi^{\prime}_{2})^{-1}
are inverses, or equivalently that

(\phi_{3}\times\phi_{1})\circ\Psi|_{T}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{2})^{-1}=(\phi^{\prime}_{3}\times\phi^{\prime}_{1})\circ\Psi|_{T}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi^{\prime}_{2})^{-1}

as claimed.

(iii)
: For each
T\in\Sch_{S}
as in
(i)
, we define
\alpha_{\mathcal{B},T}\colon(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{\mathcal{B}}},\underline{f_{\mathcal{B}}})|_{T}\to\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T}\times\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T}
by the formula

\alpha_{\mathcal{B},T}=(\phi_{3}\times\phi_{1})\circ\Psi|_{T}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{2})^{-1}.

This is of course an isomorphism and it is unique by
(ii)
. Furthermore, given two such
T_{1},T_{2}\in\Sch_{S}
, the restrictions
\alpha_{\mathcal{B},T_{1}}|_{T_{1}\times_{S}T_{2}}
and
\alpha_{\mathcal{B},T_{2}}|_{T_{1}\times_{S}T_{2}}
will each be constructed from an element of
\mathcal{P}(T_{1}\times_{S}T_{2})
. Once again by
(ii)
, the choice of these elements does not matter and therefore
\alpha_{\mathcal{B},T_{1}}|_{T_{1}\times_{S}T_{2}}=\alpha_{\mathcal{B},T_{2}}|_{T_{1}\times_{S}T_{2}}
. Thus, since there exists a cover
\{T_{i}\to S\}_{i\in I}
consisting of such
T_{i}\in\Sch_{S}
, the various isomorphisms
\alpha_{\mathcal{B},T}
glue into a global isomorphism
\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}
. It has the claimed property by construction.
∎

Given
(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})\in\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
, constructing
\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}
as in
Lemma

3.4
(iii)
above produces another object
(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{B}})\in\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
. This construction is functorial.

Lemma 3.5
.

Consider a morphism

(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})\colon(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})\to(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}})

in
\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
. Then,

(\phi_{2},\phi_{3},\phi_{1})\colon(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{B}})\to(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}})

where
\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}
and
\alpha_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
are constructed as in
Lemma

3.4
(iii)
, is another morphism in
\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
.

Proof.

We only need to check that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{\mathcal{B}}},\underline{f_{\mathcal{B}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}},\underline{f_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell(\phi_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{3}\times\phi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}}

commutes. To do so, let
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3},(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))
and let
T\in\Sch_{S}
be such that there exists a section
(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3})\in\mathcal{P}(T)
. By
Lemma

3.4
(iii)
, we can describe
\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}
over
T
by

\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}|_{T}=(\varphi_{3}\times\varphi_{1})\circ\Psi|_{T}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\varphi_{2})^{-1}.

Because we have an isomorphism
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})
between our two objects of
\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
, we will also have a section

(\phi_{1}|_{T}\circ\varphi_{1},\phi_{2}|_{T}\circ\varphi_{2},\phi_{3}|_{T}\circ\varphi_{3})\in\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3},(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{\prime},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{\prime},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{\prime},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}))(T)

and therefore

\alpha_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}|_{T}=((\phi_{3}|_{T}\circ\varphi_{3})\times(\phi_{1}|_{T}\circ\varphi_{1}))\circ\Psi|_{T}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{2}|_{T}\circ\varphi_{2})^{-1}.

These formulas then make it clear that
(\phi_{3}\times\phi_{1})|_{T}\circ\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}|_{T}=\alpha_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}|_{T}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{2})|_{T}
. Since there exists a cover
\{T_{i}\to S\}_{i\in I}
over which
\mathcal{P}
has sections, these local equalities over each
T_{i}
imply that globally we have
(\phi_{3}\times\phi_{1})\circ\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}=\alpha_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{2})
as desired, finishing the proof.
∎

Definition 3.6
.

We define an endofunctor on
\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
by

\displaystyle\Theta^{+}_{S}\colon\mathfrak{Trip}(S)

\displaystyle\to\mathfrak{Trip}(S)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})

\displaystyle\mapsto(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{B}})

\displaystyle(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})

\displaystyle\mapsto(\phi_{2},\phi_{3},\phi_{1})

on objects and morphisms respectively, where
\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}
is defined as in
Lemma

3.4
. Notationally, we may also write
\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}=\Theta^{+}_{S}(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})
.

Lemma 3.7
.

Consider the endofunctor
\Theta^{+}_{S}
of
Definition

3.6
.

(i)

The split object
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3}\in\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
is a fixed point of
\Theta^{+}_{S}
.

(ii)

\Theta^{+}_{S}
is order
3
up to equality, i.e., it satisfies
(\Theta^{+}_{S})^{3}=\Id
. In particular, it is fully faithful and surjective.

Proof.

(i)
: Let
\Theta^{+}_{S}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3})=(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}},\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}},\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}},\Psi^{\prime})
. We must argue that
\Psi^{\prime}=\Psi
. However, this is immediate since we have a global section
(\Id,\Id,\Id)\in\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3},\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3})
, and therefore by
Lemma

3.4
(iii)
we have

\Psi^{\prime}=(\Id\times\Id)\circ\Psi\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\Id)^{-1}=\Psi.

(ii)
: It is clear from the definition that
\Theta^{+}_{S}
is order
3
on morphisms. Therefore, given
(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})\in\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
and setting notation

\displaystyle\Theta^{+}_{S}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}),

\displaystyle\Theta^{+}_{S}(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{B}})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{C}})\text{, and}

\displaystyle\Theta^{+}_{S}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{C}})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}),

we only need to check that
\alpha^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}=\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}
. Since
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3}
is a fixed point of
\Theta^{+}_{S}
by
(i)
, if we have a section
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})\in\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3},(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))(T)
for some
T\in\Sch_{S}
, then we also have that

(\phi_{3},\phi_{1},\phi_{2})\in\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3},(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{C}}))(T).

Thus by
Lemma

3.4
(iii)
, locally we have

\alpha^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}|_{T}=(\phi_{2}\times\phi_{3})\circ\Psi|_{T}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{1})^{-1}

which by
Equation

15
means that
\alpha^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}}=\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}
globally as required.
∎

In light of
Lemma

3.7
(ii)
, we denote
\Theta^{-}_{S}=(\Theta^{+}_{S})^{2}
. We also define a second, more straightforward, endofunctor on
\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
. Given two algebras
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{C}
, let
\mathrm{sw}\colon\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{B}
be the switch isomorphism,
\mathrm{sw}(b,c)=(c,b)
.

Definition 3.8
.

We define an endofunctor on
\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
by

\displaystyle\Theta_{S}\colon\mathfrak{Trip}(S)

\displaystyle\to\mathfrak{Trip}(S)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})

\displaystyle\mapsto(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})

\displaystyle(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})

\displaystyle\mapsto(\phi_{1},\phi_{3},\phi_{2}).

on objects and morphisms respectively.

It is clear that this defines an order two endofunctor and that
\Theta_{S}
is fully faithful and surjective. It also satisfies the following.

Lemma 3.9
.

Consider the functor
\Theta_{S}
of
3.8
.

(i)

There are isomorphisms

\displaystyle(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi),\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi),\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi))

\displaystyle\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\Theta_{S}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3})\text{, and}

\displaystyle(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi),\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi),\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi))

\displaystyle\colon\Theta_{S}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3}

where
\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)
is as in
Lemma

2.2
. These isomorphisms are inverses.

(ii)

Also consider
\Theta^{+}_{S}
of
Definition

3.6
. We have
\Theta^{+}_{S}\circ\Theta_{S}=\Theta_{S}\circ\Theta^{-}_{S}
.

Proof.

(i)
: This follows immediately from
Corollary

2.6
and the fact that
\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)
is order
2
.

(ii)
: Let
(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})\in\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
be an object and let

\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3},(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})).

Whenever we have a section
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})\in\mathcal{P}
, we will also have a section

(\phi_{1},\phi_{3},\phi_{2})\in\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}(\Theta_{S}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3}),(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))

which by composing with the isomorphism from
(i)
means we will have a section

(\phi_{1}\circ\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi),\phi_{3}\circ\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi),\phi_{2}\circ\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi))\in\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3},(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})).

Therefore by
Lemma

3.4
(iii)
,
\Theta^{+}_{S}(\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})
will be locally described by

\displaystyle\Theta^{+}_{S}(\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})

\displaystyle=(\phi_{2}\circ\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi))\times(\phi_{1}\circ\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi))\circ\Psi\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{3}\circ\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi))^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\phi_{2}\times\phi_{1})\circ(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\times\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi))\circ\Psi\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi))^{-1}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{3})^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\phi_{2}\times\phi_{1})\circ(\mathrm{sw}\circ\Psi)\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{3})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{sw}\circ(\phi_{1}\times\phi_{2})\circ\Psi\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{3})^{-1}.

On the other hand,
\Theta^{-}_{S}(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})
will be locally described by

\Theta^{-}_{S}(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})=(\phi_{1}\times\phi_{2})\circ\Psi\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{3})^{-1}

and so
\Theta_{S}(\Theta^{-}_{S}(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))=\mathrm{sw}\circ(\phi_{1}\times\phi_{2})\circ\Psi\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{3})^{-1}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\Theta^{+}_{S}(\Theta_{S}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\Theta^{+}_{S}(\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\Theta^{-}_{S}(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))

\displaystyle=\Theta_{S}(\Theta^{-}_{S}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})),

which finishes the proof.
∎

3.C.
Stack Endomorphisms and Cohomology

Since our base scheme
S
is arbitrary, the endofunctors
\Theta^{+}_{S},\Theta_{S}\colon\mathfrak{Trip}(S)\to\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
constructed in
Section

3.B
have obvious analogues
\Theta^{+}_{T},\Theta_{T}\colon\mathfrak{Trip}(T)\to\mathfrak{Trip}(T)
for any
T\in\Sch_{S}
. These functors can be combined into endomorphisms of the gerbe
\mathfrak{Trip}
of
Definition

3.2
.

Lemma 3.10
.

Consider a morphism in
\mathfrak{Trip}

(g,\phi)\colon(T^{\prime},(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}))\to(T,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})).

Recall that
g\colon T^{\prime}\to T
is a morphism of
S
–schemes and
\phi\colon(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}})\to(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}|_{T^{\prime}})
is an isomorphism in
\mathfrak{Trip}(T^{\prime})
.

(i)

There is an equality

\Theta^{+}_{T^{\prime}}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}|_{T^{\prime}})=(\Theta^{+}_{T}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))|_{T^{\prime}}.

(ii)

(g,\Theta^{+}_{T^{\prime}}(\phi))\colon(T^{\prime},(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}))\to(T,(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}))
is a morphism in
\mathfrak{Trip}
.

(iii)

There is an equality

\Theta_{T^{\prime}}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}|_{T^{\prime}})=(\Theta_{T}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))|_{T^{\prime}}.

(iv)

(g,\Theta_{T^{\prime}}(\phi))\colon(T^{\prime},(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}))\to(T,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))
is a morphism in
\mathfrak{Trip}
.

Proof.

(i)
: First, we know that
\Theta^{+}_{T}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})=(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{B}})
and therefore

(\Theta^{+}_{T}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))|_{T^{\prime}}=(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}|_{T^{\prime}}).

Thus, we need to show that
\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}|_{T^{\prime}}=\Theta^{+}_{T^{\prime}}(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}|_{T^{\prime}})
. Let
\{T_{i}\to T\}_{i\in I}
be a cover over which
\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3}|_{T},(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))
has sections. Over any such
T_{i}
, there will be a section
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})
and by definition of
\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}
from
Lemma

3.4
(iii)
we have

\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}|_{T_{i}}=(\phi_{3}\times\phi_{1})\circ\Psi|_{T_{i}}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{2})^{-1}.

However,
\{T^{\prime}\times_{T}T_{i}\to T^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}
is a cover of
T^{\prime}
and the sheaf

\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3}|_{T^{\prime}},(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}|_{T^{\prime}}))

will have sections over this cover, namely those sections coming from restriction along the morphisms
T^{\prime}\times_{T}T_{i}\to T_{i}
. Therefore, for such a
T^{\prime}\times_{T}T_{i}
, we will have a section
(\phi_{1}|_{T^{\prime}\times_{T}T_{i}},\phi_{2}|_{T^{\prime}\times_{T}T_{i}},\phi_{3}|_{T^{\prime}\times_{T}T_{i}})
. Thus,

\displaystyle\Theta^{+}_{T^{\prime}}(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}|_{T^{\prime}})|_{T^{\prime}\times_{T}T_{i}}

\displaystyle=(\phi_{3}|_{T^{\prime}\times_{T}T_{i}}\times\phi_{1}|_{T^{\prime}\times_{T}T_{i}})\circ\Psi|_{T^{\prime}\times_{T}T_{i}}\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi_{2}|_{T^{\prime}\times_{T}T_{i}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}|_{T_{i}})|_{T^{\prime}\times_{T}T_{i}}

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}|_{T^{\prime}})|_{T^{\prime}\times_{T}T_{i}}

which means that
\Theta^{+}_{T^{\prime}}(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}|_{T^{\prime}})
and
\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}|_{T^{\prime}}
agree on a cover of
T^{\prime}
. Hence
\Theta^{+}_{T^{\prime}}(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}|_{T^{\prime}})=\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}|_{T^{\prime}}
as desired.

(ii)
: This follows immediately from
(i)
.

(iii)
: This is straightforward since the switch isomorphism is stable under base change. In particular,
(\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})|_{T^{\prime}}=\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}|_{T^{\prime}}
.

(iv)
: This follows immediately from
(iii)
.
∎

Definition 3.11
.

We define the following two endomorphisms of the gerbe
\mathfrak{Trip}
.

(i)

\begin{aligned} \Theta^{+}\colon\mathfrak{Trip}&\to\mathfrak{Trip}\\
(T,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))&\mapsto(T,\Theta^{+}_{T}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))\\
(g,\phi)&\mapsto(g,\Theta_{T^{\prime}}^{+}(\phi))\end{aligned}

(ii)
