\begin{aligned} \Theta\colon\mathfrak{Trip}&\to\mathfrak{Trip}\\
(T,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))&\mapsto(T,\Theta_{T}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))\\
(g,\phi)&\mapsto(g,\Theta_{T^{\prime}}(\phi)).\end{aligned}

It is clear from construction that
\Theta^{+}|_{\mathfrak{Trip}(T)}\colon\mathfrak{Trip}(T)\to\mathfrak{Trip}(T)
is equal to
\Theta^{+}_{T}
, and so these endomorphisms agree with our previous endofunctors on fibers. The same holds for
\Theta
, it restricts to
\Theta_{T}
on
\mathfrak{Trip}(T)
.

Proposition 3.12
.

Consider the endomorphism
\Theta^{+}
of
Definition

3.11
(i)
.

(i)

\Theta^{+}
is order three, i.e.,
(\Theta^{+})^{3}=\Id
. In particular,
\Theta^{+}
is an isomorphism.

(ii)

Viewing the automorphism sheaf of the split object
\mathbf{Aut}(S,\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3})
as
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
via
Lemma

3.3
(ii)
, the induced group automorphism

\mathbf{Aut}(\Theta^{+})\colon\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\to\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}

is
\theta^{+}
.

(iii)

\Theta
is order two and hence an isomorphism. Furthermore,
\Theta^{+}\circ\Theta=\Theta\circ\Theta^{-}
, where we define
\Theta^{-}=(\Theta^{+})^{2}
.

(iv)

Using the isomorphisms of
Lemma

3.9
(i)
, we also identify
\mathbf{Aut}(\Theta_{S}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3}))\cong\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
. Then, the induced group automorphism

\mathbf{Aut}(\Theta)\colon\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\to\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}

is
\theta
.

Proof.

(i)
: Since
\Theta^{+}
is given by
\Theta^{+}_{T}
on fibers, this follows from
Lemma

3.7
(ii)
.

(ii)
: For
T\in\Sch_{S}
, the group homomorphism
\mathbf{Aut}(\Theta^{+})(T)
will be the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}(T)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Aut}(S,\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3})(T)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Aut}(S,\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3})(T)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}(T)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\phi,\theta^{+}(\phi),\theta^{-}(\phi))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\theta^{+}(\phi),\theta^{-}(\phi),\phi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta^{+}(\phi).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta^{+}_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Hence, we see that we obtain the map
\theta^{+}\colon\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
overall.

(iii)
: Similarly as in
(i)
, the first claim holds because
\Theta
restricts to
\Theta_{T}
on fibers, each of which is order two. Likewise, the second claim follows because it holds over each fiber by
Lemma

3.9
(ii)
.

(iv)
: For
T\in\Sch_{S}
, the group homomorphism
\mathbf{Aut}(\Theta)(T)
will be the composition

\displaystyle\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}(T)

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Aut}(S,\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3})(T)\xrightarrow{\Theta}\mathbf{Aut}(S,\Theta_{S}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3}))(T)

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Aut}(S,\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3})(T)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}(T)

which sends

\displaystyle\phi

\displaystyle\mapsto(\phi,\theta^{+}(\phi),\theta^{-}(\phi))

\displaystyle\mapsto(\phi,\theta^{-}(\phi),\theta^{+}(\phi))

\displaystyle\mapsto(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\circ\phi\circ\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi),\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\circ\theta^{-}(\phi)\circ\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi),\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\circ\theta^{+}(\phi)\circ\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi))

\displaystyle\mapsto\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\circ\phi\circ\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi).

Since
\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\circ\phi\circ\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)=\theta(\phi)
by
Lemma

2.2
(iii)
, these calculations for each
T\in\Sch_{S}
show that
\mathbf{Aut}(\Theta)=\theta
as morphisms of sheaves.
∎

Corollary 3.13
.

View
H^{1}(S,\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+})
as the set of isomorphism classes of
\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
. The maps on cohomology induced by the automorphisms
\theta^{+}
and
\theta
of
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
are

\displaystyle\widetilde{\theta^{+}}\colon H^{1}(S,\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+})

\displaystyle\to H^{1}(S,\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+})

\displaystyle[(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})]

\displaystyle\mapsto[(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{B}})]

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\theta}\colon H^{1}(S,\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+})

\displaystyle\to H^{1}(S,\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+})

\displaystyle[(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})]

\displaystyle\mapsto[(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})]

respectively.

Proof.

Since we know the induced group homomorphism from
Proposition

3.12
(ii)
and
(iv)
, the claim follows by applying
Lemma

1.8
.
∎

4.
Trialitarian Algebras

In the same style as the previous section, but now considering the group
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3}
, we define groupoids and a stack of trialitarian algebras and show that we obtain categories equivalent to the groupoid or stack of
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3}
–torsors. In
[
KMRT
, §43]
, underlying a trialitarian
\mathbb{F}
–algebra is an Azumaya algebra over a degree
3
étale extension of
\mathbb{F}
. Likewise, our definition of a trialitarian algebra over a scheme
S
involves a degree
3
étale extension
L\to S
. We first define some morphisms associated to such a cover and then use those morphisms to define trialitarian algebras. Our newly defined stack of trialitarian algebras will be equivalent to both the stack of simply connected groups of type
D_{4}
as well as the stack of adjoint groups of type
D_{4}
since they are all equivalent to the stack of
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3}
–torsors by definition. In each case, we provide a concrete realization of these equivalences by defining the adjoint and simply connected group associated to a trialitarian algebra. All of our definitions in this section are inspired by their analogues in
[
KMRT
]
, with modifications made to work in our setting.

4.A.
The Definition via the Trialitarian Cover

We recall the background on finite étale covers from
Section

1.E
. In particular, recall the morphisms
\pi,\tau\colon S^{\sqcup 6}\to S^{\sqcup 3}
. Let
\mathcal{E}
be an Azumaya
\mathcal{O}|_{S^{\sqcup 3}}
–algebra, so
\mathcal{E}
is of the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}\colon\Sch_{S^{\sqcup 3}}

\displaystyle\to\Ab

\displaystyle U\sqcup V\sqcup W

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathcal{A}(U)\times\mathcal{B}(V)\times\mathcal{C}(W)

where
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
, and
\mathcal{C}
are Azumaya
\mathcal{O}
–algebras. We denote this by
\mathcal{E}=(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
\mathcal{E}=(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})
be an Azumaya
\mathcal{O}|_{S^{\sqcup 3}}
–algebra. Then

\pi_{*}(\tau^{*}(\mathcal{E}))=(\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}).

Proof.

First, it is easy to compute that

\tau^{*}(\mathcal{E})=(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{C},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}).

Next, for
U\sqcup V\sqcup W\in\Sch_{S^{\sqcup 3}}
we have that

(U\sqcup V\sqcup W)\times_{\pi}S^{\sqcup 6}=(U\sqcup V\sqcup W)\sqcup(U\sqcup V\sqcup W).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\pi_{*}(\tau^{*}(\mathcal{E}))(U\sqcup V\sqcup W)

\displaystyle=\tau^{*}(\mathcal{E})\big((U\sqcup V\sqcup W)\times_{\pi}S^{\sqcup 6}\big)

\displaystyle=\tau^{*}(\mathcal{E})\big((U\sqcup V\sqcup W)\sqcup(U\sqcup V\sqcup W)\big)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{B}(U)\times\mathcal{C}(V)\times\mathcal{A}(W)\times\mathcal{C}(U)\times\mathcal{A}(V)\times\mathcal{B}(W)

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{C})(U)\times(\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{A})(V)\times(\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B})(W)

and
\pi_{*}(\tau^{*}(\mathcal{E}))\cong(\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B})
as claimed.
∎

Now, for a degree
3
étale cover
L\to S
, recall the twisted versions of
\pi
and
\tau
, namely
\pi_{L},\tau_{L}\colon\Sigma_{2}(L)\to L
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
L\to S
be a degree
3
étale cover and let
\mathcal{E}
be an Azumaya algebra over
L
. For any
T\in\Sch_{S}
which splits
L
, we write
\mathcal{E}|_{L\times_{S}T}=(\mathcal{A}_{T},\mathcal{B}_{T},\mathcal{C}_{T})
for Azumaya
\mathcal{O}|_{T}
–algebras
\mathcal{A}_{T}
,
\mathcal{B}_{T}
, and
\mathcal{C}_{T}
. There is a morphism of
\mathcal{O}|_{L}
–algebras

\gamma_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathcal{E}\to(\pi_{L})_{*}(\tau_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}))

that is given over any
T\in\Sch_{S}
which splits
L
by

\displaystyle\gamma_{\mathcal{E}}|_{L\times_{S}T}\colon(\mathcal{A}_{T},\mathcal{B}_{T},\mathcal{C}_{T})

\displaystyle\to(\mathcal{B}_{T}\times\mathcal{C}_{T},\mathcal{C}_{T}\times\mathcal{A}_{T},\mathcal{A}_{T}\times\mathcal{B}_{T})

\displaystyle(a,b,c)

\displaystyle\mapsto((b,c),(c,a),(a,b))

where we use
Lemma

4.1
.

Proof.

If such a map exists then it is uniquely defined by the formula above since there exists a cover
\{T_{i}\to S\}_{i\in I}
which splits
L
and
\{L\times_{S}T_{i}\to L\}_{i\in I}
is also a cover. Thus, we must argue that these local descriptions are compatible and glue into a global morphism. Let
T_{1},T_{2}\in\Sch_{S}
be two schemes which split
L
, i.e.,
L\times_{S}T_{i}\cong T_{i}\sqcup T_{i}\sqcup T_{i}
. Then,
\mathcal{E}|_{T_{i}}=(\mathcal{A}_{T_{i}},\mathcal{B}_{T_{i}},\mathcal{C}_{T_{i}})
for
i=1,2
. We have a transition isomorphism

(L\times_{S}T_{1})\times_{T_{1}}T_{12}\cong T_{12}\sqcup T_{12}\sqcup T_{12}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}T_{12}\sqcup T_{12}\sqcup T_{12}\cong(L\times_{S}T_{2})\times_{T_{2}}T_{12}

which is a permutation in
\mathbb{S}_{3}(T_{12})
. Without loss of generality, it is sufficient to assume this permutation is a diagonal section of
\mathbb{S}_{3}(T_{12})
, i.e., it is a section of the presheaf
\mathbb{P}_{3}
. Further, it is sufficient to assume it is either the cycle
c=(1\;2\;3)
or the transposition
\lambda=(2\;3)
.

First, assume it is
c
. Since the pullback

c^{*}\big((\mathcal{E}|_{L\times_{S}T_{2}})|_{L\times_{S}T_{12}}\big)=c^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}},\mathcal{B}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}},\mathcal{C}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}})=(\mathcal{B}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}},\mathcal{C}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}},\mathcal{A}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}})

agrees with
(\mathcal{E}|_{L\times_{S}T_{1}})|_{L\times_{S}T_{12}}=(\mathcal{A}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}},\mathcal{B}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}},\mathcal{C}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}})
, we have equalities

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{B}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}}.

Thus, the map
c^{*}\big((\gamma_{\mathcal{E}}|_{L\times_{S}T_{2}})|_{L\times_{S}T_{12}}\big)
acts as
(b^{\prime},c^{\prime},a^{\prime})\mapsto((c^{\prime},a^{\prime}),(a^{\prime},b^{\prime}),(b^{\prime},c^{\prime}))
which clearly matches with
(\gamma_{\mathcal{E}}|_{L\times_{S}T_{1}})|_{L\times_{S}T_{12}}
after setting
a=b^{\prime}
,
b=c^{\prime}
, and
c=a^{\prime}
. Hence, the local maps agree when the transition isomorphism is
c
.

Now, assume the transition isomorphism is
\lambda
. In this case, we obtain local equalities

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{B}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}}

and the map
\lambda^{*}\big((\gamma_{\mathcal{E}}|_{L\times_{S}T_{2}})|_{L\times_{S}T_{12}}\big)
acts as
(a^{\prime},c^{\prime},b^{\prime})\mapsto((c^{\prime},b^{\prime}),(b^{\prime},a^{\prime}),(a^{\prime},c^{\prime}))
. After setting
a=a^{\prime}
,
b=c^{\prime}
, and
c=b^{\prime}
, this too clearly agrees with
(\gamma_{\mathcal{E}}|_{L\times_{S}T_{1}})|_{L\times_{S}T_{12}}
which of course acts as
(a,b,c)\mapsto((b,c),(c,a),(a,b))
. Hence, the local maps also agree when the transition morphism is
\lambda
. This means that the local morphisms agree on overlaps and glue into a global morphism with the stated property, so we are done.
∎

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
L\to S
be a degree
3
étale cover and let
\psi\colon\mathcal{E}_{1}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{E}_{2}
be an isomorphism of Azumaya algebras over
L
. Then, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi_{L})_{*}(\tau_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{1}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi_{L})_{*}(\tau_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{2}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi_{L})_{*}(\tau_{L}^{*}(\psi))}

commutes, where
\gamma_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}
are the maps of
Lemma

4.2
.

Proof.

This may be checked locally, and therefore we may assume that everything is split. In this case,
\mathcal{E}_{i}=(\mathcal{A}_{i},\mathcal{B}_{i},\mathcal{C}_{i})
and the isomorphism
\psi
consists of three isomorphisms
\psi_{\mathcal{A}}\colon\mathcal{A}_{1}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{A}_{2}
and similarly
\psi_{\mathcal{B}}
and
\psi_{\mathcal{C}}
. We know the form of
(\pi_{L})_{*}(\tau_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{i}))
from
Lemma

4.1
, from which we also see that

(\pi_{L})_{*}(\tau_{L}^{*}(\psi))=(\psi_{\mathcal{B}}\times\psi_{\mathcal{C}},\psi_{\mathcal{C}}\times\psi_{\mathcal{A}},\psi_{\mathcal{A}}\times\psi_{\mathcal{B}}).

Then, using the description of
\gamma_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}
from
Lemma

4.2
when things are split, we can simply trace an element through the diagram, yielding

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(a,b,c)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\psi_{\mathcal{A}}(a),\psi_{\mathcal{B}}(b),\psi_{\mathcal{C}}(c))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((b,c),(c,a),(a,b))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((\psi_{\mathcal{B}}(b),\psi_{\mathcal{C}}(c)),(\psi_{\mathcal{C}}(c),\psi_{\mathcal{A}}(a)),(\psi_{\mathcal{A}}(a),\psi_{\mathcal{B}}(b)))}

which justifies the claim that the diagram commutes.
∎

We can now use the morphism
\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}\colon\Sigma_{2}(L)\to\Sigma_{2}(L)
to construct a trialitarian triple.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
(\mathcal{E},\sigma,f)
be a quadratic triple of degree
8
over
L
and consider an isomorphism

\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}\colon(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma},\underline{f})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(\pi_{L})_{*}(\tau_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}))

of algebras with semi-trace. For any
T\in\Sch_{S}
which splits
L
, we write
(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})|_{L\times_{S}T}=(\mathcal{A}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{T\mathrm{qt}})
and
\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}|_{L\times_{S}T}=(\alpha_{T\mathcal{A}},\alpha_{T\mathcal{B}},\alpha_{T\mathcal{C}})
for isomorphisms

\displaystyle\alpha_{T\mathcal{A}}\colon\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{T\mathrm{qt}})

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{B}_{T\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{C}_{T\mathrm{qt}}

\displaystyle\alpha_{T\mathcal{B}}\colon\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{B}_{T\mathrm{qt}})

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{C}_{T\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{A}_{T\mathrm{qt}}

\displaystyle\alpha_{T\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{C}_{T\mathrm{qt}})

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{A}_{T\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{B}_{T\mathrm{qt}}.

Then, there is trialitarian triple over
\Sigma_{2}(L)
,

(\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{*}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})),\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{*2}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})),\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}))\in\mathfrak{Trip}(\Sigma_{2}(L))

where
\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})
is the isomorphism locally defined by

\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})|_{\Sigma_{2}(L)\times_{S}T}=(\alpha_{T\mathcal{A}},\alpha_{T\mathcal{B}},\alpha_{T\mathcal{C}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{T\mathcal{A}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{T\mathcal{B}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{T\mathcal{C}}).

Proof.

First, we check that our claim is well-defined, i.e., that locally
\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})|_{\Sigma_{2}(L)\times_{S}T}
has the appropriate domain and codomain. Because
\pi_{L}
is the standard degree
2
étale cover of
L
, when
L
is split we will have
\pi_{L}|_{L\times_{S}T}\colon(T\sqcup T\sqcup T)\sqcup(T\sqcup T\sqcup T)\to T\sqcup T\sqcup T
and

\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})|_{\Sigma_{2}(L)\times_{S}T}=\pi_{L}|_{L\times_{S}T}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{L\times_{S}T})=(\mathcal{A}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{T\mathrm{qt}}).

The Clifford algebra
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})|_{\Sigma_{2}(L)\times_{S}T})
is then

(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{T\mathrm{qt}}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{B}_{T\mathrm{qt}}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{C}_{T\mathrm{qt}}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{T\mathrm{qt}}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{B}_{T\mathrm{qt}}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{C}_{T\mathrm{qt}})).

By the definition of
\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}
, we know from
Equation

11
that

\displaystyle\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{*}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})|_{\Sigma_{2}(L)\times_{S}T})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{B}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{T\mathrm{qt}})\text{, and}

\displaystyle\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{*2}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})|_{\Sigma_{2}(L)\times_{S}T})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{C}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{T\mathrm{qt}})

so we have that
\big(\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{*}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}))\times\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{*2}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}))\big)|_{\Sigma_{2}(L)\times_{S}T}
is the
\mathcal{O}|_{T^{\sqcup 6}}
–algebra

(\mathcal{B}_{T\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{C}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{T\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{A}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{T\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{B}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{T\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{B}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{T\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{C}_{T\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{T\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{A}_{T\mathrm{qt}}).

Hence
\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})|_{\Sigma_{2}(L)\times_{S}T}
is the morphism between the appropriate
\mathcal{O}|_{T^{\sqcup 6}}
–algebras.

Now, if the isomorphism
\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})
exists, then it is uniquely defined by the formula above over those
T\in\Sch_{S}
which split
L
and hence also split
\Sigma_{2}(L)
. Therefore, we only need to show that the given local formulas are compatible with gluing and hence produce a global isomorphism as desired. Let
T_{1},T_{2}\in\Sch_{S}
be two schemes which split
L
and set
T_{12}=T_{1}\times_{S}T_{2}
. We have an isomorphism of
S
–schemes

\beta\colon(T_{1}\sqcup T_{1}\sqcup T_{1})\times_{T_{1}}T_{12}=T_{12}\sqcup T_{12}\sqcup T_{12}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}T_{12}\sqcup T_{12}\sqcup T_{12}=(T_{2}\sqcup T_{2}\sqcup T_{2})\times_{T_{2}}T_{12}

given by a permutation
\beta\in\mathbb{S}_{3}(T_{12})
. As usual, we assume that
\beta
is one of the diagonal sections
c=(1\;2\;3)
or
\lambda=(2\;3)
.

Assume first that
\beta=c
. In this case, because the pullback of
(\mathcal{E}|_{L\times_{S}T_{2}})|_{L\times_{S}T_{12}}
along
c
must agree with
(\mathcal{E}|_{L\times_{S}T_{1}})|_{L\times_{S}T_{12}}
, we obtain equalities

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{B}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}}.

The isomorphisms
(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}|_{L\times_{S}T_{1}})|_{L\times_{S}T_{12}}
and
(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}|_{L\times_{S}T_{2}})|_{L\times_{S}T_{12}}
must also be compatible along this pullback, and so we obtain commutative diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{T_{1}})|_{T_{12}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}\times\mathcal{C}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{C}_{T_{2}})|_{T_{12}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}}\times\mathcal{B}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{T_{1}\mathcal{A}}|_{T_{12}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{T_{2}\mathcal{C}}|_{T_{12}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{B}_{T_{1}})|_{T_{12}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}\times\mathcal{A}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{T_{2}})|_{T_{12}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}}\times\mathcal{C}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{T_{1}\mathcal{B}}|_{T_{12}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{T_{2}\mathcal{A}}|_{T_{12}}}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{C}_{T_{1}})|_{T_{12}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}\times\mathcal{B}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{B}_{T_{2}})|_{T_{12}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}}\times\mathcal{A}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{T_{1}\mathcal{C}}|_{T_{12}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{T_{2}\mathcal{B}}|_{T_{12}}}

Now, the pullback of
(\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})|_{\Sigma_{2}(L)\times_{S}T_{2}})|_{\Sigma_{2}(L)\times_{S}T_{12}}
along the permutation of
(T_{12})^{\sqcup 6}
corresponding to the action of
c
is

(\alpha_{T_{2}\mathcal{C}}|_{T_{12}},\alpha_{T_{2}\mathcal{A}}|_{T_{12}},\alpha_{T_{2}\mathcal{B}}|_{T_{12}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{T_{2}\mathcal{C}}|_{T_{12}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{T_{2}\mathcal{A}}|_{T_{12}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{T_{2}\mathcal{B}}|_{T_{12}})

which must agree with
(\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})|_{\Sigma_{2}(L)\times_{S}T_{1}})|_{\Sigma_{2}(L)\times_{S}T_{12}}
which is simply

(\alpha_{T_{1}\mathcal{A}}|_{T_{12}},\alpha_{T_{1}\mathcal{B}}|_{T_{12}},\alpha_{T_{1}\mathcal{C}}|_{T_{12}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{T_{1}\mathcal{A}}|_{T_{12}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{T_{1}\mathcal{B}}|_{T_{12}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{T_{1}\mathcal{C}}|_{T_{12}}).

The commutative diagrams above show precisely that these two isomorphisms agree, and so in this case we are done.

Assume now that
\beta=\lambda
. In this case we obtain equalities

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{A}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{B}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}}.

and commutative diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{T_{1}})|_{T_{12}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}\times\mathcal{C}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{T_{2}})|_{T_{12}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}}\times\mathcal{B}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{T_{1}\mathcal{A}}|_{T_{12}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sw}\circ(\alpha_{T_{2}\mathcal{A}}|_{T_{12}})}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{B}_{T_{1}})|_{T_{12}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}\times\mathcal{A}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{C}_{T_{2}})|_{T_{12}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}}\times\mathcal{A}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{T_{1}\mathcal{B}}|_{T_{12}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sw}\circ(\alpha_{T_{2}\mathcal{C}}|_{T_{12}})}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{C}_{T_{1}})|_{T_{12}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}\times\mathcal{B}_{T_{1}}|_{T_{12}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{B}_{T_{2}})|_{T_{12}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}}\times\mathcal{C}_{T_{2}}|_{T_{12}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{T_{1}\mathcal{C}}|_{T_{12}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{sw}\circ(\alpha_{T_{2}\mathcal{B}}|_{T_{12}})}

We then require compatibility between the pullback of
(\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})|_{\Sigma_{2}(L)\times_{S}T_{2}})|_{\Sigma_{2}(L)\times_{S}T_{12}}
along the action of
\lambda
, which is

(\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{T_{2}\mathcal{A}}|_{T_{12}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{T_{2}\mathcal{C}}|_{T_{12}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{T_{2}\mathcal{B}}|_{T_{12}},\alpha_{T_{2}\mathcal{A}}|_{T_{12}},\alpha_{T_{2}\mathcal{C}}|_{T_{12}},\alpha_{T_{2}\mathcal{B}}|_{T_{12}}),

and
(\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})|_{\Sigma_{2}(L)\times_{S}T_{1}})|_{\Sigma_{2}(L)\times_{S}T_{12}}
, which is still as above in the previous case. Once again, the commutative diagrams show that these two morphisms agree, so we are done.
∎

Now that we have a trialitarian triple

(\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{*}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})),\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{*2}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})),\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}))

over
\Sigma_{2}(L)
, we have two ways of generating an isomorphism

\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell\big(\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{*}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}))\big)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{*2}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}))\times\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}),

namely by applying
\Theta_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{+}
to
\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})
or by pulling
\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})
back along
\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}
. We use these in the following definition.

Definition 4.5
.

A
trialitarian algebra
(over
S
) is a triple
(L,(\mathcal{E},\sigma,f),\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})
where

(i)

L\to S
is a degree
3
étale cover,

(ii)

(\mathcal{E},\sigma,f)
is a quadratic triple over
L
where
\mathcal{E}
is a degree
8
Azumaya
\mathcal{O}|_{L}
–algebra, and

(iii)

\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}\colon(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{E},\sigma,f),\underline{\sigma},\underline{f})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(\pi_{L})_{*}(\tau_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E},\sigma,f))
is an isomorphism of algebras with semi-trace such that

\Theta_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{+}(\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}))=\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{*}(\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}))

as isomorphisms of
\mathcal{O}|_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}
–algebras, where
\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})
is the isomorphism appearing in the trialitarian triple of
Lemma

4.4
.

Example 4.6
.

Let
L=S^{\sqcup 3}
be the split degree
3
étale cover, so
\Sigma_{2}(L)=S^{\sqcup 6}
,
\pi_{L}=\pi
,
\tau_{L}=\tau
, and
\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}=\rho
. A degree
8
quadratic triple over
L
is of the form

(\mathcal{E},\sigma,f)=((\mathcal{A},\sigma_{\mathcal{A}},f_{\mathcal{A}}),(\mathcal{B},\sigma_{\mathcal{B}},f_{\mathcal{B}}),(\mathcal{C},\sigma_{\mathcal{C}},f_{\mathcal{C}})).

By applying
Lemma

4.1
while also keeping track of semi-traces, we know that

\pi_{*}(\tau^{*}(\mathcal{E},\sigma,f))\cong(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}).

The Clifford algebra of
(\mathcal{E},\sigma,f)
is

\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})=(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}})).

where we omit writing the canonical involutions and semi-traces on the Clifford algebras. An isomorphism
\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}\colon(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma},\underline{f})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\pi_{*}(\tau^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}))
is the data of three isomorphisms

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}\colon(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{\mathcal{A}}},\underline{f_{\mathcal{A}}})

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}\colon(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{\mathcal{B}}},\underline{f_{\mathcal{B}}})

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}

\displaystyle\alpha_{3}\colon(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma_{\mathcal{C}}},\underline{f_{\mathcal{C}}})

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}.

Going back up to
\Sigma_{2}(L)
, we have that

\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\pi^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}))=(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}))

and

\displaystyle\rho^{*}(\pi^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}))\times\rho^{*2}(\pi^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}).

Then,
\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{1},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{2},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{3})
and so it is clear that

\rho^{*}(\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}))=(\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{1},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{3},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{1},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{2}).

Now, we must identify
\Theta_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{+}(\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}))
. Notice that, because
\Sigma_{2}(L)=S^{\sqcup 6}
, the trialitarian triple

(\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{*}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})),\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{*2}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})),\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}))\in\mathfrak{Trip}(\Sigma_{2}(L))

is simply the data of six triples in
\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
, namely the objects

\displaystyle(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{1})

\displaystyle(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{2})

\displaystyle(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{3})

\displaystyle(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{1})

\displaystyle(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{2})

\displaystyle(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{3}).

Since any isomorphism with
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt},\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{3}\in\mathfrak{Trip}(\Sigma_{2}(L))
will also just be six different isomorphisms between the above triples and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3}\in\mathfrak{Trip}(S)
, we see that applying
\Theta_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{+}
, which produces the isomorphism from
Lemma

3.4
(iii)
, is the same as applying
\Theta_{S}^{+}
to each of the six triples. Therefore,

\displaystyle\Theta_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{+}(\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\Theta_{S}^{+}(\alpha_{1}),\Theta_{S}^{+}(\alpha_{2}),\Theta_{S}^{+}(\alpha_{3}),\Theta_{S}^{+}(\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{1}),\Theta_{S}^{+}(\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{2}),\Theta_{S}^{+}(\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha_{1}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\Theta_{S}^{+}(\alpha_{1}),\Theta_{S}^{+}(\alpha_{2}),\Theta_{S}^{+}(\alpha_{3}),\mathrm{sw}\circ\Theta_{S}^{-}(\alpha_{1}),\mathrm{sw}\circ\Theta_{S}^{-}(\alpha_{2}),\mathrm{sw}\circ\Theta_{S}^{-}(\alpha_{3})).

Thus,
(L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})=(S^{\sqcup 3},(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}),(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}))
is a trialitarian algebra if and only if
\alpha_{2}=\Theta_{S}^{+}(\alpha_{1})
and
\alpha_{3}=\Theta_{S}^{+}(\alpha_{2})
.

4.B.
The Groupoid and the Gerbe

The definition appearing below of an isomorphism between trialitarian algebras is not surprising, and with it we obtain a groupoid of trialitarian algebras. Ultimately, we will assemble these groupoids into a gerbe which is equivalent to the gerbe of
(\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3})
–torsors. Recall, over a connected scheme
T
we view
\mathbb{S}_{3}(T)
as generated by the automorphisms
\theta^{+}|_{T}
and
\theta|_{T}
of
Section

2.A
. The group
\mathbb{S}_{3}
acts on
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
within the semi-direct product by
\phi\cdot\beta=\beta\cdot\beta^{-1}(\phi)
for
\beta\in\mathbb{S}_{3}
and
\phi\in\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
.

Definition 4.7
.

Let
\mathfrak{TriAlg}(S)
be the groupoid whose

(i)

objects are trialitarian algebras
(L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})
over
S
, and whose

(ii)

morphisms are pairs
(h,\psi)\colon(L^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}})\to(L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})
where
h\colon L^{\prime}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}L
is an isomorphism of
S
–schemes and
\psi\colon\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}h^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})
is an isomorphism of quadratic triples over
L^{\prime}
such that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma^{\prime}},\underline{f^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tau_{L^{\prime}})_{*}(\pi_{L^{\prime}}^{*}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(h^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})),\underline{h^{*}(\sigma)},\underline{h^{*}(f)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tau_{L^{\prime}})_{*}(\pi_{L^{\prime}}^{*}(h^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{*}(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\underline{\sigma},\underline{f})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{*}((\tau_{L})_{*}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell(\psi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tau_{L^{\prime}})_{*}(\pi_{L^{\prime}}^{*}(\psi))}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{*}(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})}

commutes. Composition is given by
(h_{1},\psi_{1})\circ(h_{2},\psi_{2})=(h_{1}\circ h_{2},h_{2}^{*}(\psi_{1})\circ\psi_{2})
.

We designate
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}}=(S^{\sqcup 3},(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}},\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}},\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}),(\Psi,\Psi,\Psi))\in\mathfrak{TriAlg}(S)
as the split object. The notion of trialitarian algebra is stable under base change because finite étale covers, Azumaya algebras, and Clifford algebras are all stable. If
g\colon T^{\prime}\to T
is any morphism of
S
–schemes and
(L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathfrak{TriAlg}(T)
, then
T^{\prime}\times_{T}L\to T^{\prime}
will be a degree
3
étale cover of
T^{\prime}
and
(T^{\prime}\times_{T}L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}\times_{T}L},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}|_{T^{\prime}\times_{T}L})
will be a trialitarian algebra over
T^{\prime}
. We denote this by
g^{*}(L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})
or simply by
(L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})|_{T^{\prime}}
. It is then clear that the definition below produces a fibered category.

Definition 4.8
.

Let
\mathfrak{TriAlg}\to\Sch_{S}
be the fibered category defined as follows.

(i)

The objects are of the form
(T,L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})
where
T\in\Sch_{S}
is any
S
–scheme and
(L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathfrak{TriAlg}(T)
is a trialitarian algebra over
T
.

(ii)

The morphisms are pairs
(g,\phi)\colon(T^{\prime},L^{\prime},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{\prime},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}})\to(T,L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})
where
g\colon T^{\prime}\to T
is any
S
-scheme morphism and

\phi\colon(L^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}g^{*}(L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})=(T^{\prime}\times_{T}L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{T^{\prime}\times_{T}L},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}|_{T^{\prime}\times_{T}L})

is an isomorphism in
\mathfrak{TriAlg}(T^{\prime})
of trialitarian algebras over
T^{\prime}
. Composition is given by
(g_{1},\phi_{1})\circ(g_{2},\phi_{2})=(g_{1}\circ g_{2},g_{2}^{*}(\phi_{1})\circ\phi_{2})
.

(iii)

The structure functor
\mathfrak{TriAlg}\to\Sch_{S}
is given by

(T,L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})\mapsto T\text{ and }(g,\phi)\mapsto g.

By construction, the fiber over a scheme
T\in\Sch_{S}
is the groupoid
\mathfrak{TriAlg}(T)
of
Definition

4.7
. At this point, we can define a morphism of fibered categories from trialitarian triples into trialitarian algebras which is intuitively an inclusion. Recall that for
(T,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))\in\mathfrak{Trip}
we write
\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}=\Theta^{+}_{T}(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})
for the isomorphism appearing in the object
\Theta^{+}_{T}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})
and similarly
\alpha_{\mathcal{C}}=\Theta^{+2}_{T}(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})
.

Lemma 4.9
.

There is a morphism of fibered categories
\mathfrak{c}\colon\mathfrak{Trip}\to\mathfrak{TriAlg}
defined as follows.

\displaystyle(T,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))

\displaystyle\mapsto(T,T^{\sqcup 3},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}=(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}),\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}=(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}},\alpha_{\mathcal{B}},\alpha_{\mathcal{C}}))

\displaystyle(g,(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3}))

\displaystyle\mapsto\big(g,\big(h=g^{\sqcup 3},\psi=(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})\big)\big)

on objects and morphisms respectively. Furthermore, this is a faithful functor and it is essentially surjective onto objects of the form
(T^{\prime},T^{\prime\sqcup 3},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})
, i.e., those with split degree
3
étale cover.

Proof.

First, it is clear this is well-defined on objects, i.e., that
\mathfrak{c}
indeed produces objects in
\mathfrak{TriAlg}
, since
\mathfrak{c}(T,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))
matches the description of trialitarian algebras with split étale cover given in
Example

4.6
. It is clear as well from
Example

4.6
that, if
\mathfrak{c}
is a functor, then it is essentially surjective onto such trialitarian algebras.

Now, consider a morphism

(g,(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3}))\colon(T^{\prime},(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}))\to(T,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))

in
\mathfrak{Trip}
. Using the identification
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(g^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}))_{\mathrm{qt}}=g^{*}(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}})_{\mathrm{qt}})
, this means we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}})_{\mathrm{qt}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}})_{\mathrm{qt}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell(\phi_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{2}\times\phi_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})}

In turn, through the application of
\Theta^{+}_{T^{\prime}}
, this produces two more commutative diagrams with the roles of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}
,
\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}
, and
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}
cyclically permuted and
\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}
replaced with
\alpha_{\mathcal{B}}
and
\alpha_{\mathcal{C}}
as appropriate. Thus, we combine them into the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}})_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}})_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}})_{\mathrm{qt}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(g^{\sqcup 3})^{*}(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}})_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}})_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}})_{\mathrm{qt}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(g^{\sqcup 3})^{*}(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}},\alpha_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}},\alpha_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell(\phi_{1}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell(\phi_{2}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell(\phi_{3})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\phi_{2}\times\phi_{3},\phi_{3}\times\phi_{1},\phi_{1}\times\phi_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}},\alpha_{\mathcal{B}},\alpha_{\mathcal{C}})}

which is the required commutative diagram of
4.7
(ii)
, thus our proposed morphism
\mathfrak{c}(g,(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3}))
is a morphism in
\mathfrak{TriAlg}
.

Given two composable morphisms
(g,(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})
and
(g^{\prime},(\phi^{\prime}_{1},\phi^{\prime}_{2},\phi^{\prime}_{3}))
in
\mathfrak{Trip}
, their composition is

(g\circ g^{\prime},(g^{\prime*}(\phi_{1})\circ\phi^{\prime}_{1},g^{\prime*}(\phi_{2})\circ\phi^{\prime}_{2},g^{\prime*}(\phi_{3})\circ\phi^{\prime}_{3})).

After passing through
\mathfrak{c}
, the composition
\mathfrak{c}(g,(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3}))\circ\mathfrak{c}(g^{\prime},(\phi^{\prime}_{1},\phi^{\prime}_{2},\phi^{\prime}_{3}))
is

\displaystyle\big(g\circ g^{\prime},\big(g^{\sqcup 3}\circ g^{\prime\sqcup 3},(g^{\prime\sqcup 3})^{*}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})\circ(\phi^{\prime}_{1},\phi^{\prime}_{2},\phi^{\prime}_{3})\big)\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(g\circ g^{\prime},\big((g\circ g^{\prime})^{\sqcup 3},(g^{\prime*}(\phi_{1})\circ\phi^{\prime}_{1},g^{\prime*}(\phi_{2})\circ\phi^{\prime}_{2},g^{\prime*}(\phi_{3})\circ\phi^{\prime}_{3})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}\big((g,(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})\circ(g^{\prime},(\phi^{\prime}_{1},\phi^{\prime}_{2},\phi^{\prime}_{3}))\big)

and therefore
\mathfrak{c}
is a functor.

If
\mathfrak{c}(g,(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3}))=\mathfrak{c}(g^{\prime},(\phi^{\prime}_{1},\phi^{\prime}_{2},\phi^{\prime}_{3}))
for two morphisms then it is immediate that
g=g^{\prime}
and thus
\phi_{i}=\phi^{\prime}_{i}
, so
\mathfrak{c}
is faithful as claimed.

It is obvious that
\mathfrak{c}
respects the structure functors
\mathfrak{Trip}\to\Sch_{S}
and
\mathfrak{TriAlg}\to\Sch_{S}
, and since
\mathfrak{TriAlg}
is fibered in groupoids this is sufficient for
\mathfrak{c}
to be a morphism of fibered categories. This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.10
.

The fibered category
\mathfrak{TriAlg}
of
Definition

4.8
is a gerbe.

Proof.

As before, we must first convince ourselves that
\mathfrak{TriAlg}
is a stack. Beginning with the homomorphism presheaves, let
(T,\mathcal{T}_{1})=(T,L_{1},\mathcal{E}_{1\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}_{1}})
and
(T,\mathcal{T}_{2})=(T,L_{2},\mathcal{E}_{2\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}_{2}})
be two objects in the same fiber. Let
\{T_{i}\to T\}_{i\in I}
be a cover of
T
. Assume we are given two morphisms

\displaystyle(g_{1},\phi_{1}),(g_{2},\phi_{2})

\displaystyle\in\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}((T,\mathcal{T}_{1}),(T,\mathcal{T}_{2}))(T)

\displaystyle=\Hom_{\mathfrak{TriAlg}(T)}((L_{1},\mathcal{E}_{1\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}),(L_{2},\mathcal{E}_{2\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}))

which agree over all
T_{i}
. By
[
Stacks
, Tag 040L]
, the functor

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\hskip-1.72218pt\textit{o\hskip-0.86108ptm}_{\Sch_{T}}(L_{1},L_{2})\colon\Sch_{T}

\displaystyle\to\Sets

\displaystyle T^{\prime}

\displaystyle\to\Hom_{T^{\prime}}(L_{1}\times_{T}T^{\prime},L_{2}\times_{T}T^{\prime})

is an fppf–sheaf and therefore we conclude
g_{1}=g_{2}
and denote them simply as
g
. But then, we have two maps of algebras

\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\colon\mathcal{E}_{1\mathrm{qt}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}g^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{2\mathrm{qt}})
which agree locally, and so we also have
\phi_{1}=\phi_{2}
. Hence, we have a separated presheaf.

If we are given local morphisms

(g_{i},\phi_{i})\colon(L_{1}\times_{T}T_{i},\mathcal{E}_{1\mathrm{qt}}|_{L_{1}\times_{T}T_{i}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}|_{L_{1}\times_{T}T_{i}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(L_{2}\times_{T}T_{i},\mathcal{E}_{2\mathrm{qt}}|_{L_{2}\times_{T}T_{i}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}|_{L_{2}\times_{T}T_{i}})

which are compatible on overlaps, then once again
[
Stacks
, Tag 040L]
guarantees that the
g_{i}
glue into a global
g\colon L_{1}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}L_{2}
whose restriction to
T_{i}
is
g_{i}
. Therefore, the maps
\phi_{i}
are compatible local isomorphisms between the algebras
\mathcal{E}_{1\mathrm{qt}}
and
g^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{2\mathrm{qt}})
, so we also have a glued isomorphism
\phi\colon\mathcal{E}_{1\mathrm{qt}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}g^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{2\mathrm{qt}})
. It is an isomorphism of quadratic triples, but we still must check that is qualifies as an isomorphism of trialitarian algebras. Recall that by
Lemma

1.9
we have that
\Sigma_{2}(L_{1}\times_{T}T_{i})\cong\Sigma_{2}(L_{1})\times_{T}T_{i}
. The maps
\tau_{L_{1}},\pi_{L_{1}}\colon\Sigma_{2}(L_{1})\to L_{1}
are compatible with restrictions, i.e.,
\tau_{L_{1}}|_{L_{1}\times_{T}T_{i}}=\tau_{L_{1}\times_{T}T_{i}}
and likewise for
\pi_{L_{1}}
. Of course, analogous statements hold for
L_{2}
. Therefore, if we consider the diagram of
Definition

4.7
(ii)
for this case and restrict all maps to
L_{1}\times_{T}T_{i}
, we will obtain the commutative diagrams corresponding to
Definition

4.7
(ii)
for the maps
(g_{i},\phi_{i})\in\mathfrak{TriAlg}(T_{i})
. Hence, since our desired diagram commutes locally it must commute globally as well and so
(g,\phi)
is a morphism of trialitarian algebras.

Second, we must argue that
\mathfrak{TriAlg}
allows gluing of objects. Still working with a cover
\{T_{i}\to T\}_{i\in I}
in
\Sch_{S}
, assume we have objects
(T_{i},L_{i},\mathcal{E}_{i\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}_{i}})\in\mathfrak{TriAlg}(T_{i})
and gluing data isomorphisms

(g_{ij},(h_{ij},\phi_{ij}))\colon(T_{i},L_{i},\mathcal{E}_{i\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}_{i}})|_{T_{ij}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(T_{j},L_{j},\mathcal{E}_{j\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}_{j}})|_{T_{ij}}

in
\mathfrak{TriAlg}
. If we restrict just to the information of the degree
3
étale covers, i.e.,
(T_{i},L_{i})
and morphisms
(g_{ij},h_{ij})
, then we have diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{i}\times_{T_{i}}T_{ij}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(L_{j}\times_{T_{j}}T_{ij})\times_{g_{ij}}T_{ij}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{j}\times_{T_{j}}T_{ij}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{ij}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{ij}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{ij}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{ij}}

where
p
is the projection coming from the fiber product. This makes the collection of
(p\circ h_{ij},g_{ij})
a family of morphisms in the stack of finite étale covers
F\acute{E}t
of
[
Stacks
, 0BLY]
, which by
[
Stacks
, 0BLZ]
is indeed a stack. In fact, we have gluing data in this stack and therefore we have a glued degree
3
étale cover
L\to T
. Furthermore, since
L\times_{T}T_{i}\cong L_{i}
, we have a cover
\{L_{i}\to L\}_{i\in I}
of
L
. Then, the
\phi_{ij}
becomes gluing data in the stack of quadratic triples over
L
and hence there is a glued quadratic triple
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}
. Locally it is degree
8
, so it is a degree
8

\mathcal{O}|_{L}
–algebra. Since morphisms between sheaves also allow gluing and the
\phi_{ij}
are compatible with the isomorphisms
\alpha_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}|_{T_{ij}}
and
\alpha_{\mathcal{E}_{j}}|_{T_{ij}}
, we will have a glued isomorphism
\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}
over
L
. We must verify that
(L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})
is a trialitarian algebra, i.e., that is satisfies
Definition

4.5
(iii)
. However, the isomorphism
\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})
of
Lemma

4.4
is constructed by gluing local constructions, and therefore it is clear that
\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})|_{\Sigma_{2}(L_{i})}=\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}_{i}})
. Then, because
\Theta^{+}
of
Definition

3.11
(i)
is a stack morphisms and also respects base change,

\Theta^{+}_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}(\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}))|_{\Sigma_{2}(L_{i})}=\Theta^{+}_{\Sigma_{2}(L_{i})}(\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}))=\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L_{i})}^{*}(\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}))=\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{*}(\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}))|_{\Sigma_{2}(L_{i})}.

Since
\{L_{i}\cong L\times_{T}T_{i}\to L\}_{i\in I}
is a cover and

\Sigma_{2}(L_{i})\cong\Sigma_{2}(L\times_{T}T_{i})\cong\Sigma_{2}(L)\times_{T}T_{i}\cong\Sigma_{2}(L)\times_{L}L_{i},

where the second isomorphism is by
Lemma

1.9
, we see that
\{\Sigma_{2}(L_{i})\to\Sigma_{2}(L)\}_{i\in I}
is another cover. By above, the maps
\Theta^{+}_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}(\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}))
and
\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{*}(\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}))
agree locally over this cover and so we conclude that

\Theta^{+}_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}(\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}))=\rho_{\Sigma_{2}(L)}^{*}(\Xi(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})).

Thus,
(L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})
is a trialitarian algebra, meaning that
\mathfrak{TriAlg}
allows gluing. This concludes the proof that
\mathfrak{TriAlg}
is a stack.

The claim that
\mathfrak{TriAlg}
is a gerbe is now almost immediate. Given any object
(T,L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathfrak{TriAlg}
, there is a cover which splits
L
, meaning that it locally becomes of the form
(T^{\prime},T^{\prime\sqcup 3},(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}}),(\alpha,\Theta^{+}(\alpha),\Theta^{+2}(\alpha)))
, which is the image of the trialitarian triple
(T^{\prime},(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha))\in\mathfrak{Trip}(T^{\prime})
under the morphism
\mathfrak{c}
of
Lemma

4.9
. Then, since
\mathfrak{Trip}
is a gerbe by
3.3
(i)
, all such objects are locally isomorphic to
\mathfrak{c}(T^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3}|_{T^{\prime}})\cong\mathfrak{c}(S,\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3})|_{T^{\prime}}
. Thus all objects of
\mathfrak{TriAlg}
are locally isomorphic to the split object
\mathfrak{c}(S,\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3})=(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})
, hence
\mathfrak{TriAlg}
is a gerbe and we are done.
∎

Lemma 4.11
.

The automorphism sheaf of the split object
(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})\in\mathfrak{TriAlg}(S)
is

\mathbf{Aut}(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})\cong\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3}.

In particular,
\mathfrak{TriAlg}
is equivalent to the gerbe of
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3}
–torsors.

Proof.

To ease notation, let
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}=(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}},\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}},\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}})
be the split degree
8
Azumaya algebra over
S^{\sqcup 3}
. To keep track of positions, let
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}=(\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{3})
with all
\mathcal{A}_{i}=\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}
. Recall that
\tau_{S^{\sqcup 3}}=\tau
,
\pi_{S^{\sqcup 3}}=\pi
, and we have that

\tau_{*}(\pi^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{3}))=(\mathcal{A}_{2}\times\mathcal{A}_{3},\mathcal{A}_{3}\times\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{1}\times\mathcal{A}_{2})

from
Lemma

4.1
. As part of the definition of morphisms in
4.7
(ii)
, we use a canonical isomorphism

\tau_{*}(\pi^{*}(h^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})))\cong h^{*}(\tau_{*}(\pi^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})))

for an element
h\in\mathbb{S}_{3}(S)
.

To investigate this more closely, we may assume without loss of generality that we are dealing with a diagonal section and first consider the cycle
c=(1\;2\;3)\in\mathbb{S}_{3}(S)
. Then,

\displaystyle\tau_{*}(\pi^{*}(c^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{3})))

\displaystyle=\tau_{*}(\pi^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{3},\mathcal{A}_{1}))

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{A}_{3}\times\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{1}\times\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{2}\times\mathcal{A}_{3})

and

\displaystyle c^{*}(\tau_{*}(\pi^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{3})))

\displaystyle=c^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{2}\times\mathcal{A}_{3},\mathcal{A}_{3}\times\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{1}\times\mathcal{A}_{2})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{A}_{3}\times\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{1}\times\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{2}\times\mathcal{A}_{3})

so in this case the canonical isomorphism is an equality. Furthermore, this is also clearly the case for the identity permutation and
c^{2}
, i.e., the diagonal permutations in
A_{3}(S)\subset\mathbb{S}_{3}(S)
. However, now consider the transposition
\lambda=(2\;3)
. We have

\displaystyle\tau_{*}(\pi^{*}(\lambda^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{3})))

\displaystyle=\tau_{*}(\pi^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{3},\mathcal{A}_{2}))

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{A}_{3}\times\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{2}\times\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{1}\times\mathcal{A}_{3})

and

\displaystyle\lambda^{*}(\tau_{*}(\pi^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{3})))

\displaystyle=\lambda^{*}(\mathcal{A}_{2}\times\mathcal{A}_{3},\mathcal{A}_{3}\times\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{1}\times\mathcal{A}_{2})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{A}_{2}\times\mathcal{A}_{3},\mathcal{A}_{1}\times\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{3}\times\mathcal{A}_{1})

and so now the canonical isomorphism is
(\mathrm{sw},\mathrm{sw},\mathrm{sw})
. This is also the canonical isomorphism for the other diagonal sections
c\lambda
and
c^{2}\lambda
outside of
A_{3}(S)
in
\mathbb{S}_{3}(S)
.

Now, let
h\in\mathbb{S}_{3}(S)
be any diagonal section. Since we have
h^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})=\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}
,
\tau_{*}(\pi^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}))=\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}
, and
h^{*}(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})_{\mathrm{qt}})=\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})_{\mathrm{qt}}
(in fact these equations hold even for non-diagonal sections), an automorphism
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})\colon\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}\to\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}
of quadratic triples over
S^{\sqcup 3}
will give a section of
\mathbf{Aut}(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})(S)
if and only if the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})_{\mathrm{qt}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})_{\mathrm{qt}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell(\phi_{1}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell(\phi_{2}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell(\phi_{3}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\phi_{2}\times\phi_{3},\phi_{3}\times\phi_{1},\phi_{1}\times\phi_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}

commutes, where
\beta=\alpha
if
h\in A_{3}(S)
or
\beta=\mathrm{sw}\circ\alpha
if
h\notin A_{3}(S)
. In other words, this is the information of

(i)

an automorphism
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})\in\mathbf{Aut}_{\mathfrak{Trip}(S)}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3})(S)
if
h\in A_{3}(S)
, or

(ii)

an isomorphism
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})\in\mathcal{I}\hskip-1.50696pt\textit{s\hskip-0.6458pto\hskip-0.6458ptm}_{\mathfrak{Trip}(S)}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3},\Theta_{S}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3}))(S)
if
h\notin A_{3}(S)
.

Note that, in case
(i)
this means all
\phi_{i}\in\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}(S)
and we have
\phi_{2}=\theta^{+}(\phi_{1})
and
\phi_{3}=\theta^{+}(\phi_{1})
. In case
(ii)
, they are in
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}(S)\backslash\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}(S)
. Using the isomorphisms of
Lemma

3.9
(i)
, where we recall
\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)
from
Lemma

2.2
(iii)
, we have that

(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\phi_{1},\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\phi_{2},\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\phi_{3})\in\mathbf{Aut}_{\mathfrak{Trip}(S)}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3})(S)

and so
\theta^{+}(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\phi_{1})=\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\phi_{2}
and
\theta^{+}(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\phi_{2})=\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\phi_{3}
. Since an arbitrary
h\in\mathbb{S}_{3}(S)
is locally constant, after passing to a cover this discussion is sufficient to describe all of
\mathbf{Aut}(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})(S)
. The above discussion is also stable under base change and therefore describes
\mathbf{Aut}(T,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}}|_{T})
for any
T\in\Sch_{S}
as well.

Now, we define a morphism of sheaves of groups
\mathbf{Aut}(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})\to\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3}
by defining it to act as

(17)

\displaystyle\mathbf{Aut}(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})(T)

\displaystyle\to(\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3})(T)

\displaystyle(\Id_{S},h,(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3}))

\displaystyle\mapsto\begin{cases}(1,h)(\phi_{1},1)&h\in A_{3}(T)\\
(1,h)(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\phi_{1},1)&h\notin A_{3}(T)\end{cases}

for diagonal sections
h\in\mathbb{S}_{3}(T)
, which then uniquely defines the behaviour on all sections. We claim that this is an isomorphism of groups. It is sufficient to argue that it is an isomorphism between the subpresheaf of automorphisms where
h
is a diagonal section and the subpresheaf
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{P}_{3}
where
\mathbb{P}_{3}
is the constant presheaf associated to
P_{3}
. The discussion above makes it clear that it is a bijection of presheaves with an inverse given by sending

(1,h)(\phi,1)\mapsto\begin{cases}(\Id_{S},h,(\phi,\theta^{+}(\phi),\theta^{-}(\phi)))&h\in A_{3}(T)\\
(\Id_{S},h,(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\phi,\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\theta^{+}(\phi),\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\theta^{-}(\phi)))&h\notin A_{3}(T)\end{cases}

Thus, it only remains to show that it is a group homomorphism. It is sufficient to consider compositions
(\Id_{S},h_{1},(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3}))\circ(\Id_{S},h_{2},(\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\psi_{3}))
where
h_{2}
is
c
or
\lambda
.

First, consider

(\Id_{S},h_{1},(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3}))\circ(\Id_{S},c,(\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\psi_{3}))=(\Id_{S},h_{1}c,(\phi_{2}\psi_{1},\phi_{3}\psi_{2},\phi_{1}\psi_{3})).

If
h_{1}\in A_{3}(T)
, then the left hand side maps to

\displaystyle(1,h_{1})(\phi_{1},1)(1,c)(\psi_{1},1)

\displaystyle=(1,h_{1})(1,c)(\theta^{+}(\phi_{1}),1)(\psi_{1},1)

\displaystyle=(1,h_{1}c)(\theta^{+}(\phi_{1})\psi_{1},1)

\displaystyle=(1,h_{1}c)(\phi_{2}\psi_{1},1)

which is the image of the right hand side since
h_{1}c\in A_{3}(T)
also. If
h_{1}\notin A_{3}(T)
, then the left hand side maps to

\displaystyle(1,h_{1})(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\phi_{1},1)(1,c)(\psi_{1},1)

\displaystyle=(1,h_{1}c)(\theta^{+}(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\phi_{1})\psi_{1},1)

\displaystyle=(1,h_{1}c)(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\phi_{2}\psi_{1},1)

which is the image of the right hand side since
h_{1}c\notin A_{3}(T)
also.

Second, consider

(\Id_{S},h_{1},(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3}))\circ(\Id_{S},\lambda,(\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\psi_{3}))=(\Id_{S},h_{1}\lambda,(\phi_{1}\psi_{1},\phi_{3}\psi_{2},\phi_{2}\psi_{3})).

If
h_{1}\in A_{3}(T)
then the left side maps to

\displaystyle(1,h_{1})(\phi_{1},1)(1,\lambda)(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\psi_{1})

\displaystyle=(1,h_{1}\lambda)(\theta(\phi_{1}),1)(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\psi_{1},1)

\displaystyle=(1,h_{1}\lambda)(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\phi_{1}\cdot\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi),1)(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\psi_{1},1)

\displaystyle=(1,h_{1}\lambda)(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\phi_{1}\psi_{1},1)

which is the image of the right side since
h_{1}\lambda\notin A_{3}(T)
. Here we used the description of the automorphism
\theta
of
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
from
Lemma

2.2
(iii)
. If instead,
h_{1}\notin A_{3}(T)
then the left side maps to

\displaystyle(1,h_{1})(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\phi_{1},1)(1,\lambda)(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\psi_{1})

\displaystyle=(1,h_{1}\lambda)(\theta(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\phi_{1}),1)(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\psi_{1},1)

\displaystyle=(1,h_{1}\lambda)(\phi_{1}\cdot\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi),1)(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\cdot\psi_{1},1)

\displaystyle=(1,h_{1}\lambda)(\phi_{1}\psi_{1},1)

which is the image of the right side since
h_{1}\lambda\in A_{3}(T)
. Therefore the proposed isomorphism is a group homomorphism and we are done.
∎

Corollary 4.12
.

The morphism
\mathfrak{c}\colon\mathfrak{Trip}\to\mathfrak{TriAlg}
of
Lemma

4.9
is a morphism of gerbes which sends the split object to the split object, i.e.,
\mathfrak{c}(S,\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{3})=(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})
. The corresponding induced homomorphism between automorphism sheaves is the inclusion

\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3}.

Under the corresponding map on cohomology
H^{1}(S,\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+})\to H^{1}(S,\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3})
, the images of the classes

\displaystyle[\big(S,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})\big)],

\displaystyle[\big(S,\big(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\Theta^{+}_{S}(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})\big)\big)],\text{ and }

\displaystyle[\big(S,\big(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\Theta_{S}(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})\big)\big)]

are all equal.

Proof.

It is a morphism between gerbes by
Lemma

3.3
(i)
and
Lemma

4.10
. The fact that the homomorphism between automorphism sheaves is as claimed is clear from the definition of
\mathfrak{c}
, the isomorphism described in
Lemma

3.3
(ii)
, and the description of the automorphism sheaf in
Lemma

4.11
. The objects
\mathfrak{c}(S,(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\Theta^{+}_{S}(\alpha_{\mathcal{A}})))
and
\mathfrak{c}(S,(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{qt}},\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{A}}))
are isomorphic in
\mathfrak{TriAlg}(S)
via the map
(\Id_{S},c=(1\;2\;3),(\Id_{\mathcal{B}},\Id_{\mathcal{C}},\Id_{\mathcal{A}}))
. This shows that the images of the first and second cohomology classes are equal. Similarly, the map
(\Id_{S},\lambda=(2\;3),(\Id_{\mathcal{A}},\Id_{\mathcal{C}},\Id_{\mathcal{B}}))
will be an isomorphism in
\mathfrak{TriAlg}(S)
, showing that the images of the first and third classes are equal.
∎

5.
Groups of Type
D_{4}

In this final section, we define the simply connected and adjoint groups associated to a trialitarian algebra. These constructions will provide equivalences of stacks between the stack of simply connected groups of type
D_{4}
and
\mathfrak{TriAlg}
or the stack of adjoint groups of type
D_{4}
and
\mathfrak{TriAlg}
. Our definition of the adjoint groups is a direct analogue of the definition in
[
KMRT
, 44.A]
and our approach to the simply connected groups is heavily motivated by
[
KMRT
, 44.A]
.

5.A.
Adjoint Groups

The adjoint groups of type
D_{4}
are exactly the twisted forms of
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
. It is therefore clear that the stack of all such groups, which we denote
\mathfrak{D}_{4}^{\mathrm{ad}}
and which has

(i)

objects which are pairs
(T,\mathbf{G})
where
T\in\Sch_{S}
is any scheme and
\mathbf{G}
is a sheaf of groups on
\Sch_{T}
for which there exists a cover
\{T_{i}\to T\}_{i\in I}
such that
\mathbf{G}|_{T_{i}}\cong\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}|_{T_{i}}
,

(ii)

morphisms are pairs
(g,\varphi)\colon(T^{\prime},\mathbf{G}^{\prime})\to(T,\mathbf{G})
where
g\colon T^{\prime}\to T
is any morphism in
\Sch_{S}
and
\varphi\colon\mathbf{G}^{\prime}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}g^{*}(\mathbf{G})
is an isomorphism of groups, and

(iii)

structure functor
(T,\mathbf{G})\mapsto T
and
(g,\varphi)\mapsto g
,

is a gerbe which is equivalent to the gerbe of
\mathbf{Aut}(\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+})=\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3}
–torsors.

We now define a subgroup of the automorphisms of a trialitarian algebra. Recall that for a trialitarian algebra
(T,L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathfrak{TriAlg}(T)
, the sections of its automorphism sheaf over
T^{\prime}\in\Sch_{T}
are of the form
(g=\Id_{T^{\prime}},h,\psi)
where
h\colon L\times_{T}T^{\prime}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}L\times_{T}T^{\prime}
is an automorphism in
\Sch_{T^{\prime}}
and
\psi\colon\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}h^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})
is an automorphism of quadratic triples which respects
\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}}
.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
(T,\mathcal{T})=(T,L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathfrak{TriAlg}
be a trialitarian algebra over
T\in\Sch_{S}
. We define the group
\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}}
to be the subgroup
\mathbf{Aut}_{\mathfrak{TriAlg}}(T,\mathcal{T})
consisting of automorphisms which fix
L
. In detail, for
T^{\prime}\in\Sch_{T}

\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}}(T^{\prime})=\{(\Id_{T^{\prime}},h,\psi)\in\mathbf{Aut}_{\mathfrak{TriAlg}}(T,\mathcal{T})(T^{\prime})\mid h=\Id_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}}\}.

Lemma 5.2
.

With the definition of
\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}}
of a trialitarian algebra given above, we have the following.

(i)

\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}}}\cong\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
.

(ii)

A morphism
(g,\phi)\colon(T^{\prime},\mathcal{T}^{\prime})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(T,\mathcal{T})
in
\mathfrak{TriAlg}
induces an isomorphism

\displaystyle\phi^{\mathrm{ad}}\colon\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}g^{*}(\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}})=\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{g^{*}(\mathcal{T})}

\displaystyle\varphi

\displaystyle\mapsto\phi\circ\varphi\circ\phi^{-1}

of group sheaves over
T^{\prime}
.

(iii)

Given
(T,\mathcal{T})\in\mathfrak{TriAlg}
, the group
\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}}
is a twisted form of
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}|_{T}
.

Proof.

(i)
: It is clear that the automorphism of
Lemma

4.11
, in particular
Equation

17
, restricts to an isomorphism
\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
.

(ii)
: The morphism
(g,\phi)
consist of a scheme morphism
g\colon T^{\prime}\to T
and an isomorphism
\phi\colon\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\to g^{*}(\mathcal{T})
in
\mathfrak{TriAlg}(T^{\prime})
. Therefore, if
\varphi
is an automorphism of
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
in
\mathfrak{TriAlg}(T^{\prime})
, it is clear that
\phi\circ\varphi\circ\phi^{-1}
is an automorphism of
g^{*}(\mathcal{T})
. Furthermore, let
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=(L^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}})
and
\mathcal{T}=(L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})
. Then,
g^{*}(\mathcal{T})=(L\times_{T}T^{\prime},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})
and the isomorphism
\phi
contains a component
h\colon L^{\prime}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}L\times_{T}T^{\prime}
which is a isomorphism of
T^{\prime}
–schemes. Any
\varphi\in\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}
acts as the identity on
L^{\prime}
, so it is clear that
\phi\circ\varphi\circ\phi^{-1}
acts as
h\circ\Id_{L^{\prime}}\circ h^{-1}=\Id_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}}
on
L\times_{T}T^{\prime}
and therefore
\phi\circ\varphi\circ\phi^{-1}\in\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{g^{*}(\mathcal{T})}
. Hence, the given morphism is well-defined. It is clear it is an isomorphism and so we are done.

(iii)
: Since
\mathfrak{TriAlg}
is a gerbe by
Lemma

4.10
, the object
(T,\mathcal{T})
is locally isomorphic to
(T,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}}|_{T})
, i.e., there is a cover
\{T_{i}\to T\}_{i\in I}
over which
(T_{i},\mathcal{T}|_{T_{i}})\cong(T_{i},\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}}|_{T_{i}})
. Therefore by
(ii)
, there are isomorphisms
\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{(\mathcal{T}|_{T_{i}})}\cong\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{(\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}}|_{T_{i}})}
, where this second group is
\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{(\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}}|_{T_{i}})}=(\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}}})|_{T_{i}}\cong\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}|_{T_{i}}
by
(i)
. This shows that
\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}}
is a twisted form of
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}|_{T}
.
∎

Lemma

5.2
shows that the groups
\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}}
are adjoint group of type
D_{4}
and that we have a functor

(18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{ad}}\colon\mathfrak{TriAlg}

\displaystyle\to\mathfrak{D}_{4}^{\mathrm{ad}}

\displaystyle(T,\mathcal{T})

\displaystyle\mapsto(T,\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}})

\displaystyle(g,\phi)

\displaystyle\mapsto(g,\phi^{\mathrm{ad}}).

It is a morphism of stacks since it is clearly compatible with the structure functors and
\mathfrak{D}_{4}^{\mathrm{ad}}
is a gerbe.

Theorem 5.3
.

The morphism
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{ad}}
above is an equivalence of gerbes.

Proof.

We argue using
Lemma

1.7
. Consider the object
(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})\in\mathfrak{TriAlg}(S)
. Recall that by
Lemma

4.11
, its automorphism group is
\mathbf{Aut}_{\mathfrak{TriAlg}}(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})\cong\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3}
. By
Lemma

5.2
(i)
, we have
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{ad}}(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})=\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
which is the obvious subgroup of
\mathbf{Aut}_{\mathfrak{TriAlg}}(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})
. By
Equation

12
, the automorphism group of
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
is
\mathbf{Aut}_{\mathfrak{D}_{4}^{\mathrm{ad}}}(\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+})\cong\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3}
. The morphism
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{ad}}
induces a group homomorphism between automorphism groups

\mathbf{Aut}(\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{ad}})\colon\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3}\to\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3}.

Applying
Lemma

5.2
(ii)
, we see that for
\phi\in\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3}
, its image under this group homomorphism is the automorphism

\displaystyle\mathbf{Aut}(\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{ad}})(\phi)\colon\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}

\displaystyle\varphi

\displaystyle\mapsto\phi\varphi\phi^{-1}.

However, by the description of
\mathbf{Aut}_{\mathfrak{D}_{4}^{\mathrm{ad}}}(\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+})
in
Equation

12
, the elements of
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\subset\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3}
represent the inner automorphisms of
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
, and therefore
\mathbf{Aut}(\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{ad}})
acts as the identity on this subgroup. Furthermore, for
\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{3}
and
\varphi\in\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
we have

\sigma\varphi\sigma^{-1}=(\sigma\sigma^{-1})\cdot\sigma(\varphi)=\sigma(\varphi)

and so
\sigma
maps to itself as an automorphism of
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}
, i.e.,
\mathbf{Aut}(\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{ad}})
is the identity on
\mathbb{S}_{3}
also. This makes it clear that
\mathbf{Aut}(\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{ad}})
is an isomorphism and so by
Lemma

1.7
we conclude that
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{ad}}
is an equivalence as claimed.
∎

5.B.
Simply Connected Groups

The simply connected groups of type
D_{4}
are exactly the twisted forms of
\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
. Therefore, the stack denoted
\mathfrak{D}_{4}^{\mathrm{sc}}
consisting of

(i)

objects which are pairs
(T,\mathbf{G})
where
T\in\Sch_{S}
is any scheme and
\mathbf{G}
is a sheaf of groups on
\Sch_{T}
for which there exists a cover
\{T_{i}\to T\}_{i\in I}
such that
\mathbf{G}|_{T_{i}}\cong\mathbf{Spin}_{8}|_{T_{i}}
,

(ii)

morphisms which are pairs
(g,\varphi)\colon(T^{\prime},\mathbf{G}^{\prime})\to(T,\mathbf{G})
where
g\colon T^{\prime}\to T
is any morphism in
\Sch_{S}
and
\varphi\colon\mathbf{G}^{\prime}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}g^{*}(\mathbf{G})
is an isomorphism of groups, and

(iii)

structure functor which sends
(T,\mathbf{G})\mapsto T
and
(g,\varphi)\mapsto g

is a gerbe which is equivalent to the gerbe of
\mathbf{Aut}(\mathbf{Spin}_{8})=\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3}
–torsors.

Given a trialitarian algebra
(T,\mathcal{T})=(T,L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathfrak{TriAlg}
, the quadratic triple
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}
has an associated spin group as in
Section

1.C.i
, namely
\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}}\subset\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})
. The quadratic triple
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}
also has its orthogonal group,
\mathbf{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}}\subset\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{\times}
and we have the vector representation
\chi_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}}\to\mathbf{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}}
. Thus, we have the following two compositions from
\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}}
into
(\pi_{L})_{*}(\tau_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}))^{\times}
,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})^{\times}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi_{L})_{*}(\tau_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}))^{\times}\textnormal{ and}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}}\subset\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}^{\times}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi_{L})_{*}(\tau_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}))^{\times}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi_{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\mathcal{E}}}

where
\gamma_{\mathcal{E}}
is the map of
Lemma

4.2
. Both these maps are group homomorphisms and thus their equalizer is a group sheaf over
L
, which we denote as

\displaystyle\mathbf{preSpin}_{\mathcal{T}}\colon\Sch_{L}

\displaystyle\to\Grp

\displaystyle L^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mapsto\{a\in\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}}(L^{\prime})\mid\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}(a)=(\gamma_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\chi_{\mathcal{E}})(a)\}.

After a pushforward, it is the group we are after.

Definition 5.4
.

Let
(T,\mathcal{T})=(T,L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})\in\mathfrak{TriAlg}
be a trialitarian algebra over
T\in\Sch_{S}
and let
f\colon L\to T
be the degree
3
étale cover. We define the spin group of the trialitarian algebra to be
\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}}=f_{*}(\mathbf{preSpin}_{\mathcal{T}})
. It is a group sheaf over
T
.

We keep in mind that there is seemingly some ambiguity in the definition of pushforwards coming from the non-uniqueness of fiber products. Given a trialitarian algebra
(T,\mathcal{T})=(T,L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})
and a scheme
T^{\prime}\in\Sch_{T}
, we may have two fiber products
L^{\prime}
and
L\times_{T}T^{\prime}
. Of course, these come with an isomorphism
L^{\prime}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}L\times_{T}T^{\prime}
of
T
–schemes due to their universal properties. This isomorphism yields an isomorphism of groups
\mathbf{preSpin}_{\mathcal{T}}(L\times_{T}T^{\prime})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{preSpin}_{\mathcal{T}}(L^{\prime})
. When discussing the pushforward, we view this isomorphism as an identification and abusively write that
\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}}(T^{\prime})=\mathbf{preSpin}_{\mathcal{T}}(L\times_{T}T^{\prime})=\mathbf{preSpin}_{\mathcal{T}}(L^{\prime})
.

Lemma 5.5
.

With the definition of
\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}}
of a trialitarian algebra given above, we have the following.

(i)

\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}}}\cong\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
.

(ii)

Let
(g,\phi)\colon(T^{\prime},\mathcal{T}^{\prime})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(T,\mathcal{T})
be a morphism in
\mathfrak{TriAlg}
. In detail,
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=(L^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}})
,
\mathcal{T}=(L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})
, and
\phi=(h,\psi)
where
h\colon L^{\prime}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}L\times_{T}T^{\prime}
is an isomorphism of
T^{\prime}
–schemes and
\psi\colon\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}h^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})
is an isomorphism of quadratic triples over
L^{\prime}
which respects the trialitarian algebra structures. This induces an isomorphism of group sheaves over
L^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mathbf{preSpin}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}h^{*}(\mathbf{preSpin}_{\mathcal{T}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\psi)(a)

whose pushforward is an isomorphism of group sheaves over
T^{\prime}

\phi^{\mathrm{sc}}\colon\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}g^{*}(\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}}).

(iii)

Given
(T,\mathcal{T})\in\mathfrak{TriAlg}
, the group
\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}}
is a twisted form of
\mathbf{Spin}_{8}|_{T}
.

Proof.

(i)
: In the case of the split trialitarian algebra

(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})=(S,S^{\sqcup 3},\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}=(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}},\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}},\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}),\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}=(\Psi,\Psi,\Psi))

we know that

\pi_{*}(\tau^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}))\cong(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}},\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}},\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}\times\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}})

from
Lemma

4.1
. Also, since
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})=(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}))
it is clear that
\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}}=(\mathbf{Spin}_{8},\mathbf{Spin}_{8},\mathbf{Spin}_{8})
. From the calculations in
Lemma

2.3
, we know that for
(a,b,c)\in\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}}
we have

\displaystyle(\Psi,\Psi,\Psi)(a,b,c)

\displaystyle=
