\displaystyle\big(\big(\chi(\theta^{+}(a)),\chi(\theta^{-}(a))\big),\big(\chi(\theta^{+}(b)),\chi(\theta^{-}(b))\big),\big(\chi(\theta^{+}(c)),\chi(\theta^{-}(c))\big)\big).

On the other hand, since
S^{\sqcup 3}
is the split degree
3
étale cover of
S
we know the explicit description of
\gamma_{\mathcal{E}}
, and so

(\gamma_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\chi_{\mathcal{E}})(a,b,c)=\gamma_{\mathcal{E}}(\chi(a),\chi(b),\chi(c))=\big((\chi(b),\chi(c)),(\chi(c),\chi(a)),(\chi(a),\chi(b))\big).

Therefore,
(a,b,c)\in\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}}}
if and only if the relations

\displaystyle\chi(\theta^{+}(a))

\displaystyle=\chi(b)

\displaystyle\chi(\theta^{-}(a))

\displaystyle=\chi(c)

\displaystyle\chi(\theta^{+}(b))

\displaystyle=\chi(c)

\displaystyle\chi(\theta^{-}(b))

\displaystyle=\chi(a)

\displaystyle\chi(\theta^{+}(c))

\displaystyle=\chi(a)

\displaystyle\chi(\theta^{-}(c))

\displaystyle=\chi(b)

hold. If these relations hold, then
\theta^{+}(a)=xb
for some
x\in\Ker(\chi)
. In turn, this means that
\theta^{-}(a)=\theta^{+}(x)\theta^{+}(b)
and so
\chi(\theta^{-}(a))=\chi(\theta^{+}(x))\chi(\theta^{+}(b))
. However, we have both
\chi(\theta^{-}(a))=\chi(c)
and
\chi(\theta^{+}(b))=\chi(c)
from the relations and so we must also have
\chi(\theta^{+}(x))=1
, i.e.,
\theta^{+}(x)\in\Ker(\chi)
also.

From the discussion in the proof of
Proposition

1.5
, we recall that for
T^{\prime}\in\Sch_{T}
we have
\Ker(\chi)(T^{\prime})=\{h_{e_{3}-e_{4}}(t)h_{e_{3}+e_{4}}(t)\mid t\in\boldsymbol{\mu}_{2}(T^{\prime})\}
where

h_{\alpha}(t)=w_{\alpha}(t)w_{\alpha}(-1)=x_{\alpha}(t)x_{-\alpha}(-t^{-1})x_{\alpha}(t)x_{\alpha}(-1)x_{-\alpha}(1)x_{\alpha}(-1)

for a root
\alpha
and
t\in\mathcal{O}(T^{\prime})^{\times}
as in
[
Ste68
, Lemma 19]
. Using the description of the automorphism
\theta^{+}
of
\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
from
Lemma

2.1
, we can compute that
\theta^{+}(h_{e_{3}-e_{4}}(t))=h_{e_{3}-e_{4}}(t)
and that

\displaystyle\theta^{+}(h_{e_{3}+e_{4}}(t))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x_{-e_{1}-e_{2}}(-t)x_{e_{1}+e_{2}}(t^{-1})x_{-e_{1}-e_{2}}(-t)x_{-e_{1}-e_{2}}(1)x_{e_{1}+e_{2}}(-1)x_{-e_{1}-e_{2}}(1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle w_{-e_{1}-e_{2}}(-t)w_{-e_{1}-e_{2}}(1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle w_{-e_{1}-e_{2}}(-t)w_{-e_{1}-e_{2}}(-1)w_{-e_{1}-e_{2}}(1)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{-e_{1}-e_{2}}(-t)h_{-e_{1}-e_{2}}(-1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{-e_{1}-e_{2}}(t).

Here we use the facts that
w_{\alpha}(t)^{-1}=w_{\alpha}(-t)
, that
h_{\alpha}(t)
is multiplicative in
t
, and
[
Rue20
, Lemma 1(3)]
which states that in spin groups we have the relation
w_{\alpha}(t)^{2}=h_{\alpha}(-1)
. Thus,
\theta^{+}(h_{e_{3}-e_{4}}(t)h_{e_{3}+e_{4}}(t))
is not in
\Ker(\chi)
unless
t=1
and hence
h_{e_{3}-e_{4}}(t)h_{e_{3}+e_{4}}(t)=1
also.

Therefore, we conclude that
\theta^{+}(a)=b
and, by the same argument, that
\theta^{+}(b)=c
. It is then clear that the map

\displaystyle\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}}}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Spin}_{8}

\displaystyle(a,b,c)

\displaystyle\mapsto a

is an isomorphism with inverse
a\mapsto(a,\theta^{+}(a),\theta^{-}(a))
.

(ii)
: First, we show that we have the claimed isomorphism over
L^{\prime}
between
\mathbf{preSpin}
groups. Let
X\in\Sch_{L^{\prime}}
. By definition we have that

\mathbf{preSpin}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}(X)=\{a\in\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}}(X)\mid\alpha_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}(a)=(\gamma_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\circ\chi_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}})(a)\}.

Next, we have

\displaystyle h^{*}(\mathbf{preSpin}_{\mathcal{T}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})(X)=

\displaystyle\{a\in\mathbf{Spin}_{h^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})}(X)\mid h^{*}(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})(a)=\big(h^{*}(\gamma_{\mathcal{E}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})\circ h^{*}(\chi_{\mathcal{E}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})\big)(a)\}.

Since we are given the isomorphism
\psi\colon\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}h^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})
, we have the induced isomorphism between the Clifford algebras which in turn gives the functoriality of
\mathbf{Spin}
, so we have a group isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{qt}}}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Spin}_{h^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})}

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\psi)(a).

We must check that this isomorphism restricts to our desired isomorphism between
\mathbf{preSpin}
groups. Since
\psi
is coming from an isomorphism of trialitarian algebras, i.e.,
(h,\psi)
is a morphism in
\mathfrak{TriAlg}(T^{\prime})
, we have that

h^{*}(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\psi)=(\tau_{L})_{*}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\psi))\circ\alpha_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}.

By combining
Lemma

1.3
and
Lemma

4.3
, we have that

h^{*}(\gamma_{\mathcal{E}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})\circ h^{*}(\chi_{\mathcal{E}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\psi)=(\tau_{L})_{*}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\psi))\circ\gamma_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\circ\chi_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}.

Therefore, if
a\in\mathbf{preSpin}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}(X)
, then

\displaystyle\big(h^{*}(\gamma_{\mathcal{E}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})\circ h^{*}(\chi_{\mathcal{E}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\psi)\big)(a)

\displaystyle=\big((\tau_{L})_{*}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\psi))\circ\gamma_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\circ\chi_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\big)(a)

\displaystyle=\big((\tau_{L})_{*}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\psi))\big)((\gamma_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\circ\chi_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}})(a))

\displaystyle=\big((\tau_{L})_{*}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\psi))\big)(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}(a))

\displaystyle=\big((\tau_{L})_{*}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\psi))\circ\alpha_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\big)(a)

\displaystyle=\big(h^{*}(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})\circ\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\psi)\big)(a)

which shows that
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\psi)(a)\in h^{*}(\mathbf{preSpin}_{\mathcal{T}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})(X)
as desired.

Finally, it is left to show that the pushforward of
h^{*}(\mathbf{preSpin}_{\mathcal{T}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})
along
f^{\prime}\colon L^{\prime}\to T^{\prime}
is equal to
g^{*}(\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}})
. Given
Y\in\Sch_{T^{\prime}}
, we have
g^{*}(\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}})(Y)=\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}}(Y)=\mathbf{preSpin}_{\mathcal{T}}(L\times_{T}Y)
. However, we have a diagram of schemes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{\prime}\times_{T^{\prime}}Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\times_{T}Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\times_{T}{T^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

and so

\displaystyle\mathbf{preSpin}_{\mathcal{T}}(L\times_{T}Y)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{preSpin}_{\mathcal{T}}(L^{\prime}\times_{T^{\prime}}Y)

\displaystyle=h^{*}(\mathbf{preSpin}_{\mathcal{T}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})(L^{\prime}\times_{T^{\prime}}Y)

\displaystyle=f^{\prime}_{*}\big(h^{*}(\mathbf{preSpin}_{\mathcal{T}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})\big)(Y)

showing that
g^{*}(\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}})=f^{\prime}_{*}\big(h^{*}(\mathbf{preSpin}_{\mathcal{T}}|_{L\times_{T}T^{\prime}})\big)
. Thus setting
\phi^{\mathrm{sc}}=f^{\prime}_{*}(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\psi))
produces our desired isomorphism.

(iii)
: This follows from
(i)
since any trialitarian algebra
(T,\mathcal{T})
is locally isomorphic to
(T,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}}|_{T})
because
\mathfrak{TriAlg}
is a gerbe by
Lemma

4.10
.
∎

As a result of
Lemma

5.5
, it is immediate that we have a morphism of stacks

(19)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{sc}}\colon\mathfrak{TriAlg}

\displaystyle\to\mathfrak{D}_{4}^{\mathrm{sc}}

\displaystyle(T,\mathcal{T})

\displaystyle\mapsto(T,\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}})

\displaystyle(g,\phi)

\displaystyle\mapsto(g,\phi^{\mathrm{sc}}).

Theorem 5.6
.

The morphism
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{sc}}
above is an equivalence of gerbes.

Proof.

We argue using
Lemma

1.7
. We focus on the object
(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})\in\mathfrak{TriAlg}(S)
, which by
Lemma

5.5
(i)
is mapped by
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{sc}}
to
\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
. In particular,
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{sc}}(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})=\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
via the isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}}}(X)=\{(a,\theta^{+}(a),\theta^{-}(a))\in\mathbf{Spin}_{8}(X)^{3}\}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Spin}_{8}(X)

\displaystyle(a,\theta^{+}(a),\theta^{-}(a))

\displaystyle\mapsto a.

We know that
\mathbf{Aut}_{\mathfrak{TriAlg}}(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})\cong\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3}
by
4.11
and we also know that
\mathbf{Aut}_{\mathfrak{D}_{4}^{\mathrm{sc}}}(\mathbf{Spin}_{8})\cong\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3}
by
Equation

12
. Therefore,
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{sc}}
gives us an induced group homomorphism

\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{sc}}_{(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})}\colon\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3}\to\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3}

which we argue is an isomorphism.

The automorphism group of
(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}}|_{T})
takes the following form, as in
4.11
. By definition
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}}=(S^{\sqcup 3},(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}},\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}},\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}),(\Phi,\Phi,\Phi))
. The elements
\phi\in\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}(S)\subset(\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3})(S)
act on the algebra by

(\phi,\theta^{+}(\phi),\theta^{-}(\phi))\colon(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}},\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}},\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}},\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}},\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}).

The elements of
\mathbb{S}_{3}(S)
permute the components of
S^{\sqcup 3}
. Let
\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{3}(S)
be a diagonal section. If
\sigma\in A_{3}(S)\subset\mathbb{S}_{3}(S)
, then it also cyclically permutes the three components of the algebra. However, if
\sigma\notin A_{3}(S)
, the it both permutes the factors of the algebra according to
\sigma
and applies the automorphism
(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi),\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi),\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi))
where
\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)\in\mathbf{PGO}_{8}(S)
is the element of
Lemma

2.2
(iii)
.

The induced action on the Clifford algebra is similar. A section
\phi\in\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}(S)
acts by
\big(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\theta^{+}(\phi)),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\theta^{-}(\phi))\big)
on
(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O})_{\mathrm{qt}}))
. The diagonal sections of
A_{3}(S)
simply permute the factors of the Clifford algebra while the other diagonal sections of
\mathbb{S}_{3}(S)
both permute the factors and apply
(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)))
. However, by the commutativity of
Equation

6
and by
Lemma

2.2
(i)
, we have that

(20)

(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\pi_{\mathbf{O}}(\varphi)))|_{(\mathbf{Spin}_{8},\mathbf{Spin}_{8},\mathbf{Spin}_{8})}=(\theta,\theta,\theta).

As a penultimate step, we point out that the commutativity of
Equation

6
implies that for
\phi\in\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}(S)
, if we restrict the induced automorphism
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi)
of the Clifford algebra to
\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
, we obtain the usual action of
\phi
as an inner automorphism of
\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
, i.e. viewing
\phi\in\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}(S)\subseteq\mathbf{Aut}(\mathbf{Spin}_{8})
. Indeed, locally there exist sections
x\in\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
lying above
\phi
, and
\phi=\Inn(x)
as an automorphism of
\mathbf{Spin}_{8}
. However,
Equation

6
shows that
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi)=\Inn(x)
as an inner automorphism of the Clifford algebra, and this justifies our claim.

Finally, we can describe
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{sc}}_{(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})}
. A section
\phi\in\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}(S)
viewed as a subset of
\mathbf{Aut}_{\mathfrak{TriAlg}}(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})(S)
acts on the Clifford algebra by
\big(\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\theta^{+}(\phi)),\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\theta^{-}(\phi))\big)
, which by the previous paragraph restricts to the automorphism
(\phi,\theta^{+}(\phi),\theta^{-}(\phi))
on
(\mathbf{Spin}_{8},\mathbf{Spin}_{8},\mathbf{Spin}_{8})
. Thus, taking the projection onto the first factor we see that
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{sc}}_{(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})}(\phi)=\phi
. Now we consider diagonal sections of
\mathbb{S}_{3}(S)
. For the cycle
c=(1\;2\;3)\in A_{3}(S)\subset\mathbb{S}_{3}(S)
, it acts as a permutation on the Clifford algebra and thus also on
(\mathbf{Spin}_{8},\mathbf{Spin}_{8},\mathbf{Spin}_{8})
, sending

(a,\theta^{+}(a),\theta^{-}(a))\mapsto(\theta^{+}(a),\theta^{-}(a),a).

Taking the first projection, this shows that
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{sc}}_{(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})}(c)=\theta^{+}
. Lastly, the transposition
\lambda=(2\;3)\in\mathbb{S}_{3}(S)
acts on
(\mathbf{Spin}_{8},\mathbf{Spin}_{8},\mathbf{Spin}_{8})
by transposing the last two positions and applying the automorphism of
Equation

20
. It thus sends

(a,\theta^{+}(a),\theta^{-}(a))\mapsto\big(\theta(a),\theta(\theta^{-}(a)),\theta(\theta^{+}(a))\big).

Considering the first factor, we obtain that
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{sc}}_{(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})}(\lambda)=\theta
. In summary,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{sc}}_{(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})}(S)\colon(\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3})(S)

\displaystyle\to(\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{S}_{3})(S)

\displaystyle\phi

\displaystyle\mapsto\phi

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle\mapsto\theta^{+}

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle\mapsto\theta

for
\phi\in\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}(S)
and
c,\lambda
diagonal as above. This discussion is independent of the base and therefore also describes how
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{sc}}_{(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})}(T)
acts over any
T
when the section of
\mathbb{S}_{3}(T)
is diagonal. It is thus clear that
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{sc}}_{(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})}
restricts to an automorphism of the subpresheaf
\mathbf{PGO}_{8}^{+}\rtimes\mathbb{P}_{3}
and therefore
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{sc}}_{(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})}
is an isomorphism itself. Hence, we are done.
∎

Remark 5.7
.

As one would expect, give a trialitarian algebra
(T,\mathcal{T})\in\mathfrak{TriAlg}
, there is an isogeny of groups
\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}}
. Let
\mathcal{T}=(L,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}})
and let
X\in\Sch_{T}
. We know by definition that
\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}}(X)\subset\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}}(L\times_{T}X)
. Any element of
\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}}(X)
is an automorphism of
(L\times_{T}X,\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{L\times_{T}X},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}|_{L\times_{T}X})
which fixes
L\times_{T}X
and can therefore be viewed as simply a quadratic triple isomorphism of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}|_{L\times_{T}X}
, i.e.,
\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}}(X)\subset\mathbf{PGO}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}}(X)
. In fact, since
\phi
respects
\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}
, we have
\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}}(X)\subset\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}}(X)
. We can then consider the standard isogeny

\displaystyle\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}}

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}}

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle\mapsto\Inn(\chi_{\mathcal{E}}(a)).

Let
a\in\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}}
and set
\phi=\Inn(\chi_{\mathcal{E}}(a))
. By the commutativity of
Equation

6
, we know that
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\phi)=\Inn(a)
as an automorphism of
\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})
. Locally,
\chi_{\mathcal{E}}(a)
is an element of the form
(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})\in(\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}),\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}),\Mat_{8}(\mathcal{O}))
and one can easily compute that
(\tau_{L})_{*}\big(\pi_{L}^{*}\big(\Inn(\chi_{\mathcal{E}}(a))\big)\big)=\Inn\big(\gamma_{\mathcal{E}}(\chi_{\mathcal{E}}(a))\big)
holds locally, and thus globally as well. However, by assumption
(\gamma_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\chi_{\mathcal{E}})(a)=\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}(a)
and therefore the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tau_{L})_{*}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-1.29167pt\ell(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tau_{L})_{*}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{qt}}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.90417pt\ell(\phi)=\Inn(a)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tau_{L})_{*}(\pi_{L}^{*}(\phi))=\Inn(\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}(a))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\mathcal{E}}}

commutes, meaning that
\phi\in\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}}
. For the split trialitarian algebra
(S,\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}})
, we recover the usual isogeny
\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}}}\cong\mathbf{Spin}_{8}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{8}\cong\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{split}}}
. Since this is the description locally, we see that in general
\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}}
is surjective and the kernel is the center. Because of this, an automorphism of
\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}}
induces an automorphism of
\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}}
and thus we get a morphism of stacks

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{4}^{\mathrm{sc}}

\displaystyle\to\mathfrak{D}_{4}^{\mathrm{ad}}

\displaystyle(T,\mathbf{Spin}_{\mathcal{T}})

\displaystyle\mapsto(T,\mathbf{PGO}^{+}_{\mathcal{T}})

which makes the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}_{4}^{\mathrm{sc}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{TriAlg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{D}_{4}^{\mathrm{ad}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{sc}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{ad}}}

commute up to equality.
